#2876. Nvidia hires law firm Wachtell Lipton to lead Jensen Huang affair probe

Nvidia's board of directors said on Tuesday that it hired Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to conduct an investigation of CEO Jensen Huang and whether company secrets or national security was compromised, after Huang admitted to having an affair with a woman who has been connected to Chinese intelligence.

"The independent members of the NVIDIA Board have retained former acting U.S. Attorney of the Southern District of New York Joon H. Kim and his firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to conduct a thorough and independent investigation as was announced on Sunday," the board said in a statement.

Kim joined the firm last month, and he was previously the chief U.S. prosecutor in Manhattan, according to Law360.

Nvidia has been thrust into turmoil since Huang's acknowledgement of the affair over the weekend. The chipmaker's stock dropped 6% on Tuesday after falling more than 4% on Monday, as investors worry that the investigation and scandal could result in the co-founder and CEO being forced out.

Nvidia and the board are facing pressure from critics and politicians over what the company knew about sales of its restricted AI chips to Chinese companies.

Over the past few weeks, The New York Times and Financial Times have reported that U.S. intelligence agencies surveilled an Asian technology executive, Alice Huang, who has been affiliated with Nvidia customer Megaspeed. These agencies have concluded that she is passing information to Beijing and is involved with Chinese intelligence, according to the reports.

Huang has become the face of the AI industry, going by his first name "Jensen" to customers and rivals as Nvidia's technology is used to train nearly all major AI models. The chipmaker has become an essential source of financing for AI startups and Huang has met with presidents and CEOs around the world as Nvidia's public face.

But Huang's actions have thrown the chipmaker into instability as he admitted to an affair with Alice Huang in an email to staff on Sunday.

"My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did," Huang wrote in the message, while emphasizing that he is an American and supports U.S. national security.

An Nvidia representative said at the time that Huang was committed to continuing as CEO of Nvidia without interruption.

On Sunday, Nvidia's board said that it would investigate the allegations that Huang had been the target of a foreign intelligence effort.

Nvidia's board also said on Sunday that it has no reason to believe either national security nor Nvidia secrets were compromised.

#2873. Singapore wants a bigger slice of the global gold market. Is it too late to the party?

Singapore is stepping up efforts to become a major player in the global gold market, betting that a new central bank vaulting service and tax incentives will help it attract more bullion flows.

The Monetary Authority of Singapore on Tuesday launched a gold vaulting service for foreign central banks and sovereign entities, with an initial capacity of 500 metric tons.

The city-state also plans to introduce tax exemptions for certain wholesale gold-related services such as vaulting, refining, recasting, testing and broking. In June, Singapore unveiled plans for a physical gold clearing system to facilitate the settlement of bullion transactions in the city-state, with operations expected to begin in the first quarter of 2027.

The initiatives mark the country's latest attempt to establish itself as a key bullion hub alongside established centers such as London and Switzerland, as more gold trading activity shifts toward Asia.

But breaking into a market dominated by established trading hubs may prove an uphill battle. For instance, London vaults held about 9,850 metric tons of gold at the end of October, including commercial and central bank storage.

The push from Singapore also comes as rival Hong Kong is also expanding its gold storage and clearing infrastructure, leveraging its proximity to China, the world's largest gold market.

"Singapore is very late to the party as building up a physical hub requires volume when it comes to trade backed structures," said Juerg Kiener, chief investment officer at Swiss Asia Capital.

"The largest gold market is China and its window to the rest of the world is Hong Kong," he told CNBC, adding that Singapore is not well-positioned to compete with Hong Kong in gold-backed trade.

Kiener said Singapore could instead carve out a niche in physical gold storage for financial institutions, although commercial vaulting services are already available in the city-state, including at its high-security freeport near Changi Airport.

Unlike commercial vaults, the new facility will be operated by Singapore's central bank, offering foreign monetary authorities an alternative sovereign custodian for their gold.

"It's not something that we see happening all that often in the gold market or in the central bank community," said Shaokai Fan, global head of central banks at the World Gold Council.

The move comes as central banks increasingly reassess where they store their bullion amid geopolitical tensions, said Fan. Over the past 12 months, several countries have diversified gold storage, with many planning to do so, according to a World Gold Council report published in September.

Singapore's political stability, strong legal framework and extensive air connectivity could make it an attractive alternative to traditional storage centers, he added.

Nikolas Kavalis, managing director at Metals Focus, said the sovereign vaulting service represents an important addition to Singapore's existing gold infrastructure, but cautioned that storage alone would not be sufficient to establish a fully functioning bullion market.

The bigger challenge is building sufficient liquidity and trading activity to compete with London, which remains the dominant global over-the-counter gold market.

Both Singapore and Hong Kong are still developing their local clearing infrastructure, Kavalis said, meaning neither has yet established the kind of deep trading ecosystem that underpins London's dominance.

Attracting central bank gold could help Singapore build that liquidity, particularly if bullion stored locally can also be mobilized for transactions rather than simply held in custody.

Singapore's efforts come as gold flows have already undergone a significant transformation over the past decade.

Historically, much of the world's bullion moved through London and Switzerland, where large gold bars were traded or refined before being shipped to Asian markets.

But the expansion of Asian refineries and growing regional demand have increasingly allowed gold to move directly between Asian markets, reducing their dependence on European trading centers, Kavalis said.

The development of more sophisticated gold hubs in Asia could accelerate that shift.

Still, Hong Kong might have an edge in serving central banks and institutions whose financial activities are closely tied to China, while Singapore could appeal to those already active in its banking and financial markets, Kavalis said.

Fan sees room for both centers to grow. "We see the two centers complementing each other and not really competing with each other," he said.

Singapore's Deputy Prime Minister Gan Kim Yong similarly dismissed suggestions that the two financial hubs were looking at securing a larger share of the same pie. "It's not a zero-sum game," Gan said Tuesday, adding that Singapore's vaulting service would provide central banks with another option for diversifying where they hold their reserves.

Whether Singapore can translate its reputation as a trusted financial center into meaningful bullion flows remains to be seen. "It's a bit chicken and egg because to attract volumes you want liquidity, but to get liquidity you need volumes," Fan said.

#2872. Top Wall Street analysts find these 3 stocks attractive as long-term investments

Investors have been grappling with stock market volatility, weighing elevated oil prices, persistent geopolitical tensions, the Federal Reserve's rate hike and a debate about AI safety. For all that, it helps to look beyond short-term noise and focus on stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors gain clarity, as these experts conduct in-depth research to pick stocks with attractive growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Enterprise software and cloud infrastructure company Oracle (ORCL) recently announced better-than-expected results for the first quarter of fiscal 2027. The quarter benefited from solid growth in cloud infrastructure revenue.

After hosting a series of investor meetings with Ken Bond, Oracle head of investor relations following Q1 earnings, Guggenheim analyst John DiFucci reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $400. Oracle remains a Guggenheim Best Idea, he said.

"We continue to believe ORCL is a decade stock, driven by the massive (and profitable) opportunity ahead for AI training and inferencing," said DiFucci. He also cited the traditional Public Cloud business and additional AI-enabled cloud database and cloud applications for his bullish stance. DiFucci expects all the factors to drive revenue and profit acceleration over the next few years.

While Oracle delivered 850 MW of data center capacity in Q1 FY27, Bond cautioned that Infrastructure as a Service revenue in any quarter depends more on the timing of when that capacity comes online, than on the headline MW number. DiFucci expects IaaS growth to stay strong, with full-year growth expected above Q1 FY27's 120% constant currency rate. Management expects total revenue growth to accelerate sequentially throughout fiscal 2027, as the faster-growing infrastructure business makes up a larger share of the mix.

DiFucci addressed concerns regarding customer concentration, saying OpenAI's share of total remaining performance obligations has dropped from 80% last June to about 50% now, and is expected to keep falling as new contracts are signed with new and existing customers.

DiFucci ranks No. 263 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 62% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17.5%. See Oracle Insider Trading Activity on TipRanks.

Space systems and launch services provider Rocket Lab (RKLB) was recently initiated as a buy with an $80 price target by Raymond James analyst Brian Gesuale.

"RKLB has assembled launch, spacecraft, components, payloads and optical communications, with Neutron and Iridium completing medium-lift and applications/spectrum in 2027," said Gesuale.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that with the completion of the Iridium acquisition, Rocket Lab will become the only vertically integrated space company with positive free cash flow (FCF). The deal will add a deployed constellation valued at more than $3 billion, globally coordinated L-band spectrum and about $500 million in earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA) and $300 million in free cash flow (FCF).

Gesuale highlighted RKLB's strong revenue and backlog growth and progress toward positive EBITDA and FCF in 2027/2028. Rocket Lab is moving from a heavy investment phase into monetization, with estimated revenue growth of 59% in 2026, the analyst said, noting that RKLB's backlog has more than doubled to almost $2.4 billion from $1.1 billion in Q3 2025.

Given strong growth projections, the analyst expects RKLB's gross margins to expand from about a 35% trough in 2027 to about 47% by 2030, while R&D spending is estimated to decline from ~35% of sales in 2026 to about 10% in 2028, creating substantial operating leverage as the platform matures.

Gesuale ranks No. 369 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of about 17%. See Rocket Lab Statistics on TipRanks.

J.P. Morgan analyst Doug Anmuth recently upgraded Meta Platforms (META) to buy from hold and raised his price target to $820 from $640. Despite the year-to-date underperformance of META, the 5-star analyst still sees significant upside, as Meta is in the early stages of launching frontier models and AI-powered offerings beyond advertising, particularly the Muse AI agent and Meta Model API access.

"Frontier models are at the core of Meta's product and monetization pipeline over a multi-year period, and its path toward superintelligence," said Anmuth.

In the summer of 2025, Meta set a goal to rebuild its Meta Superintelligence Labs team and deliver frontier AI models within a year, the analyst said. The team appears to have met its goal, rapidly advancing from the rollout of Muse Spark 1.1 in July to Muse Spark 1.3, which is competitive with Claude and GPT models, Anmuth said.

Meanwhile, Meta's Watermelon product will expand opportunities in consumer products, business intelligence, and Family of Apps engagement, the analyst said. The product will enhance internal operations and overall efficiency, said Anmuth, who's confident of Meta's ability to bring consumer-driven AI products to its extensive user base of 4 billion and views its distribution network as a competitive advantage.

While Meta has focused on frontier models and AI product development, Anmuth also sees solid opportunity in its AI infrastructure capacity, which he believes will support future compute needs.

Anmuth ranks No. 832 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of about 9.40%. See Meta Platforms Financials on TipRanks.

#2871. The Number Crunch: Demolition Derby at Gillette Stadium

Not much to say about what happened on Sunday, other than a complete trouncing. The Patriots' had minimal push back in what was a rout of New England at the hands of the Green Bay Packers.

Let's go underneath the hood of the Patriots' Week 9 defeat.

New England's All-Pro cornerback's dreadful performance was the most glaring issue in this loss. Christian Gonzalez, the 24 year-old who just signed a record-setting four-year, $135 million deal, was the first one to tell the media he had a "bad game." But that might be an understatement, as this was his worst performance in his young NFL career.

According to NextGen Stats, Gonzalez allowed 10 receptions on 11 targets for 187 yards, which was easily the most he has ever given up in one game. In fact, Gonzalez nearly gave up more yardage in this game than in his first six combined this season, in which he allowed 196 yards.

He also unfortunately made history, becoming the first defender since at least 2018 to let up 80 or more yards to two receivers in the same game. Christian Watson (171 yards), and Matthew Golden (164 yards), each had career highs in receiving yards, combining for 335 of Jordan Love's 372 passing yards.

The Patriots have yet to win a game in which they force no turnovers in 2026, and are now 0-3 under that circumstance. New England has the exact same record when Drake Maye throws two or more interceptions in a game, tossing three more against Green Bay. His 11 interceptions through the first nine weeks of the NFL season, tied for the league lead alongside Justin Herbert, are the most for a Patriots' QB to open a season since Drew Bledsoe in 1994.

While he did pick up his play in the second half versus the Packers, the Patriots were already in a 17-point hole when he got the ball in the third quarter. His mistakes plus the defense allowing 31 points in the first 34 minutes and 9 seconds all contributed to the game's blowout nature.

A familiar limitation also showed itself again, adding to a game full of flaws on the New England side. Right when it felt that it was snowballing for the Patriots, it became an avalanche when Rhamondre Stevenson got stripped by Isaiah McDuffie midway through the third quarter. This is only Stevenson's second fumble on the year, the other one occurring in Week 2 against the Pittsburgh Steelers. In fact, his fumbling issues have gotten progressively better.

Since his alarming 2024 season, when he fumbled times, he only has five combined in 2025 and 2026. The second half begun with a Packers touchdown, followed by a Patriots punt, a Packers field goal, then the fumble by Stevenson. The Packers followed the turnover up with a one-play, 39 yard touchdown to Christian Watson, planting the momentum on the Green Bay sideline.

In three straight games, the Patriots have had to play from behind in the first half, having sluggish starts against the Bears, Dolphins, and now the Packers. New England began on Sunday down 17-0 after the celebration of the 2016 Patriots' Super Bowl team, not scoring their first points until 9:23 on the clock in the 2nd quarter. New England was also down 13-3 at halftime in Miami, and losing 10-0 at the conclusion of the first quarter up in Chicago. The Patriots have been outscored in the first half 51-37 in the past three games.

Patriots' head coach Mike Vrabel was asked about coming out skittish following the 45-27 loss to the Packers.

"It's tough to spot good teams any sort of lead," claimed Vrabel. "And we've done that."

The Patriots will have a long trip across the pond to Munich, Germany to figure out how to correct their numerous mistakes before they face the Detroit Lions. While the Lions' defense is not as strong as Green Bay's, their offense has fire power in Jahmyr Gibbs and Amon-Ra St. Brown. New England will need a strong gameplan to neutralize those two if they want to get back on track in Week 10.

#2870. U.S., China should ‘harbor no illusions’ as they cooperate, compete on AI, Bessent says

The U.S. and China should "harbor no illusions" about their differences as they engage to contain the risks of artificial intelligence, while competing with each other at the same time, Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent said.

Bessent said in prepared remarks to the National Committee on U.S.-China Relations in New York Tuesday that the two governments had established a new dialogue mechanism on "super intelligence," or SI, aimed at guarding against "shared risks" and averting any "misunderstanding between our two systems."

The mechanism will see both sides exchanging "best practices" for testing advanced models before they are released, and "keeping human judgement firmly in command of the technology," Bessent said, expecting both sides to alert each other to serious incidents involving the SI models.

Bessent, however, stopped short of making any commitment to limit advancement of the technology, as Beijing and Washington remain locked in an intense race to achieve global dominance in AI.

The Trump administration has largely resisted broadening restrictions on AI development over concerns of ceding a competitive edge to China, though the president has more recently signaled some openness to putting in place regulatory safeguards.

"I ​don't mind guardrails," he said in a CBS interview released Sunday, adding that ⁠he did not want regulations to put the U.S. in "a deep second position" behind China, ​which he ​said ⁠had no such restrictions.

China's foreign ministry in September dismissed calls to slow down frontier models, saying that fearmongering and confrontation would only disrupt global AI governance. Huiyao Wang, founder of Center for China and Globalization, a think tank that closely aligns with Beijing's thinking, said last month that the U.S.-China talks should "confront AI risks and manage them together."

During Xi's September visit, Washington and Beijing agreed to launch an AI dialogue with a communications channel to help defuse serious incidents. The two leaders are expected to meet for the third time next week, with Bessent joining, on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation Leaders' Meeting from Nov. 18 to 19.

As the technology advances, the two nations will need to demonstrate how they can "reconcile the demands of competition with the responsibilities of leadership," Bessent said in his Tuesday speech. "Our interests will not always coincide, and our approach to every challenge likewise will not always converge," he added.

Washington had briefly suspended access by foreign nationals to Anthropic's Mythos and Fable 5 models in June before restoring it weeks later. The U.S. government has also restricted exports of the most advanced chips to Beijing, and accused Chinese developers of copying American models through what it calls industrial-scale distillation.

Xie Feng, China's ambassador to the U.S., told the audience at the gala that efforts by both countries were "indispensable" to addressing issues from global flashpoints to "improving artificial intelligence governance." Cooperation "may not resolve every challenge," he said, but "none can be resolved without it."

The event was organized at a time when controversy around Nvidia Corp CEO Jensen Huang's affair with an alleged Chinese spy has been making headlines.

The billionaire tech executive, who joined Trump's delegation to Beijing in May and was invited to the state dinner in Washington in September, is at the center of a major corporate and national security scandal after admitting to an extramarital affair with a Chinese businesswoman whom the U.S. intelligence officials suspect of espionage.

While Huang's presence at the upcoming summit has been thrown into doubt, other business leaders such as Tesla and SpaceX CEO Elon Musk and Mastercard's Michael Miebach could be on Trump's delegation next week, according to South China Morning Post.

#2868. Genoa's Jaxon Magnone consummate target at tight end

Jaxon Magnone is like an African safari rolled into one football player.

Initially, Magnone was like a wobbly baby giraffe for Genoa. All height; very limited coordination.

He finished his career as a rhinoceros at defensive end. In between, he matured to 6-foot-4 and 215 pounds. He learned to leverage and block like a gorilla might. He's strong and fast like the cats. He can be graceful and agile, like a gazelle at tight end, with the vertical jump to overcome crocodiles in any crowd.

He didn't play varsity as a giraffe, err, 14-year old freshman linebacker. He was a cornerback as a sophomore and safety as a junior. He played defensive end and safety in the same game as a senior. As a freshman, Magnone was basically too tall, too soon. He grew only about another inch the next three years.

"He just needed that one year to grow into his body and understand how to use his body to dominate," Comets coach Bill Fisher said. "He worked on the right things; footwork, coordination. You see the results."

High school football The Scott Stecher standard is brave new world for Woodmore football

He was twice recognized offensive player of the year in the Northern Buckeye Conference. He was first-team on defense both seasons, at different positions. He was first-team on offense as a sophomore.

"Even in pictures, how little I looked," he said. "I'm the same height, but I'm surprised how far I came physically. I was a big baseball kid. As a freshman, I thought I'd just be a receiver, a tight end in our offense. But a skinny kid, running decent routes. Block when needed."

Magnone embraced the transitions and was inquisitive while buying in. He enjoyed the opportunity afforded by multiple position groups to get to know more teammates. He wanted to be coached: "What do I do in this scenario?"

"A talent like that, you want him in the best position to help the team," Fisher said. "He has the size and ability to play anywhere. That was just a thing that was. But to want to be good and play at another level — he initiated coaching points. That was a breath of fresh air."

Magnone wasn't running from any blocks prior, but defensive end was a physical adjustment. He had to learn to move and strike with his hands, his head and his shoulders. It made him a better tight end.

Fisher believes players are supposed to make plays. That makes him difficult to impress. He remembers three of Magnone's receptions, even though none went for a score. He split defenders and tracked a long pass against Triway in the postseason as a junior.

He had a one-handed snare and concentrated for another hands-only catch in heavy traffic against Eastwood.

Magnone and Genoa quarterback Myles Mollenhauer have been friends since before they started playing football. Their bond was forged on the playground in elementary school. Then, it was mutually honed for football specifics.

"I couldn't ask for a better guy to throw me the ball," Magnone said. "The love and trust we have. 'It's a slant.' With a look, that's what we were running and what we were throwing. That was the trust built over the years."

Magnone also had an impact when he didn't touch the ball. He occasionally played wildcat quarterback or tailback in I-formation. He could line up out wide as a receiver. He was used off the line for jet sweeps and motion. As a decoy. He liked to think he was a threat to a defense because his teammates were threats. And vice-versa.

"Alex Materni, Tommy Braun, Hunter Bickel, Gavin Grigson," he said. "We have such great players. It allows me to do what I do. I wouldn't be who I am or the player I am without my family, coaches and friends."

Magnone continues his career at Valparaiso, a Division I FCS program.

"They believe in me as a player, as a person, academics," he said. "I fell in love with the atmosphere and the faith they have in me to come in and play. I'm excited to go to the next level."

Magnone established Genoa records for touchdown receptions in a career (28), receiving yards in a career (3,077), receiving yards in a season (1,030), receptions in a career (189) and catches in a season (72). He was third-team all-state as a junior and second-team as a sophomore.

"He can make any kind of catch from any kind of body position," Fisher said. "He's the consummate target. He's the real deal in the game of football."

You don't spot many players like Magnone in the high school habitat. He's rare to see.

#2867. Jim Cramer says Anthropic's explosive growth could justify a record-breaking IPO

Anthropic's stunning revenue growth helps justify its $1 trillion-plus valuation ahead of what could become the largest initial public offering in history, CNBC's Jim Cramer said Tuesday.

The Claude developer filed updated IPO paperwork Tuesday outlining plans to offer 825 million shares at $70 to $78 apiece. At the high end, the offering would raise $64.35 billion and value Anthropic at up to $1.405 trillion on a fully diluted basis. The company plans to price its shares Nov. 18 and begin trading the following day. Throw in a separate $26 billion private placement purchased by leading AI companies including Nvidia and Alphabet, and Anthropic's entire haul could exceed SpaceX's raise from its June IPO.

"The fact that this deal is on is bullish for tech and the AI trade," the "Mad Money" host said.

Anthropic reported $33.2 billion in revenue during the first nine months of 2026, up 1,206% from a year earlier, according to its updated prospectus. Much of that growth has been driven by enterprise customers, particularly surging adoption of Claude Code, its AI coding agent. "Anthropic's tantalizing growth rate is the key reason why this should be the largest IPO in history," Cramer said.

The company now has more than 11,900 customers spending over $100,000 annually, up from roughly 1,500 at the end of 2025. Existing customers are also spending dramatically more, with Anthropic reporting a net revenue retention rate of approximately 900% at the end of September, meaning customers are spending roughly nine times as much as a year earlier.

While Anthropic is seeing accelerating revenue growth through the first nine months of the year, the company's quarter-over-quarter growth rate seemed to peak in the second quarter, slowing to 47% in the third quarter from 144% in Q2. "To some extent, it's the law of large numbers at work, and let's be clear that 47% sequential growth is still very impressive," Cramer said.

While some deceleration is inevitable as the company gets larger, he said that increased competition from OpenAI and the possibility that customers are beginning to scrutinize their AI spending could also be factors. One thing is for sure, Anthropic won't have to jockey for position with OpenAI, which pushed its IPO plans into next year.

Additionally, Anthropic's annualized revenue run rate, which projects yearly revenue based on the most recent month's sales, suggests demand remains strong. The frontier AI lab reported an annualized revenue run rate of $81 billion at the end of October, putting the company within striking distance of $100 billion by year-end if its current momentum continues.

"It's good to see that it's still growing nicely," Cramer said. "As for Anthropic's revenue and growth rate, the main subject of tonight's review, it's nothing less extraordinary, even if we are slightly past the peak, growth-wise."

#2866. Rams more likely to miss the playoffs than make the playoffs, according to betting odds

The Rams entered the season as the overwhelming favorites to win the Super Bowl. As the dust settles on the NFL trade deadline, the Rams are now not even favored to make the playoffs.

The latest betting odds have the Rams as -130 favorites to miss the playoffs, and +110 underdogs to make the playoffs.

That represents a massive shift from before the season, when the Rams were -500 favorites to make the playoffs, the best playoff odds of any team in the league.

The Rams are a disappointing 5-4, and if the playoffs started today they'd be a game behind the Packers for the third and final NFC playoff spot. They're also 2.5 games behind the 49ers in the NFC West race.

Perhaps more importantly, injuries are mounting for the Rams, with the latest big one being that wide receiver Puka Nacua is heading to injured reserve, where he'll join Davante Adams, as Matthew Stafford is running out of healthy receivers.

If the Rams get healthy and make the playoffs, they'll be a major threat to any postseason opponent, which is why they still have the seventh-best odds in the league to win the Super Bowl at +1300.

But as the 49ers pull away in the NFC West, and the NFC wild card race looks increasingly competitive, the Rams' chances of being in it in January are dwindling.

#2865. Homeowners tap equity as HELOC balances post biggest jump since 2005: New York Fed report

Homeowners tapped their home equity lines of credit in the third quarter at the highest rate in more than two decades, according to a new Federal Reserve Bank of New York report on household debt released Tuesday.

Balances on HELOCs jumped by $23 billion during the three months ending Sept. 30, the largest increase since 2005. That brings the outstanding amount owed to $481 billion, the report shows. This also marks the 18th consecutive quarterly increase.

HELOC balances peaked at roughly $714 billion in the first quarter of 2009, according to a 2015 New York Fed report.

The current rise in balances comes amid a sluggish housing market, due at least partly to the so-called mortgage rate lock-in effect. Just under half of outstanding mortgages — 49.1% — carried rates of 4% or lower in the second quarter, according to a Realtor.com analysis of federal mortgage data, leaving many owners reluctant to give up their low rates to purchase another home at current higher borrowing costs.

The median price of an existing home was $417,500 in September, according to the National Association of Realtors, a real estate trade group. While up just 1.3% from a year earlier, the amount is nearly 54% higher than in September 2019, according to NAR. The average rate on a typical 30-year fixed-rate mortgage is 7.3% as of Tuesday, according to Mortgage News Daily.

"HELOCs let them borrow against their equity without giving up the low interest rate on their home," said certified financial planner Douglas Boneparth, president and founder of Bone Fide Wealth in New York City and a member of CNBC's Financial Advisor Council.

Mortgage holders collectively had $17.5 trillion in home equity as of the third quarter, according to a new report from Intercontinental Exchange, or ICE, a financial markets technology and data company.

Tappable equity that can be accessed — meaning a homeowner's equity would remain at or above 20% of the home's value after tapping it — rose 1.2%, or $16,600, from a year ago to an average of $204,000 per borrower, the ICE report shows.

"Homeowners are sitting on substantial housing wealth, with 2.4 million mortgage holders now holding at least $1 million in equity," Andy Walden, head of mortgage and Housing Market Research at ICE, said in the report.

HELOCs let you tap a line of credit over time as you need the money, instead of getting it all at once like a home equity loan or cash-out refinance.

While HELOCs may have fewer up-front costs than a home equity loan, they generally have a variable interest rate. This means it will move up and down based on a benchmark like the prime rate, which banks use as a basis to set rates on a variety of loans. And, while the Federal Reserve doesn't control that rate, it is influenced by changes the Fed makes to the so-called federal funds rate.

"If your budget only works at today's rate, then your budget doesn't really work," Boneparth said of HELOCs. "You kind of have to be mindful of the fact that rates may go up."

The average interest rate for a $30,000 HELOC was 7.49% as of Nov. 4, according to Bankrate.

Many HELOCs also have a "draw" period when you can take money out, which often lasts five or 10 years. During that time, you typically are only required to pay the interest on any funds you've withdrawn. After that, however, you'll enter a repayment period of, say, 10 or 20 years, when you're required to pay both interest and principal. Because of that, your payments will jump if you've only been paying interest.

"The real test comes when the repayment period kicks in, and the principal starts to show up, if that's how their HELOC is structured," Boneparth said. "Payments potentially could double overnight, and you really want to know that before you sign anything."

Additionally, consider what you're planning to use the money for, Boneparth said.

"If you're borrowing against your home to improve your home, that could make a lot of sense," Boneparth said. "Borrowing against your house to pay off credit cards is a little trickier because you're turning unsecured debt into debt secured by the roof over your head."

In that case, if your spending habits don't change, he said, "you're just moving the problem somewhere a little scarier."

#2864. NBA issues retroactive flagrant foul to Keldon Johnson after officials missed his blatant groin kick of Devin Booker

Keldon Johnson got away with shenanigans in real time. But the NBA's not letting him off the hook.

The NBA announced Tuesday that it's issued a postgame Flagrant Foul 1 to the San Antonio Spurs guard for an infraction during Monday's blowout win over the Phoenix Suns.

The infraction? He kicked Devin Booker in the groin, very clearly so. Booker went down to the court in obvious pain. But game officials chose to move along as if nothing happened.

Here's the incriminating video the Suns were happy to post on their social media Tuesday.

That isn't subtle. Referee Natalie Sago was standing with a clear view of the infraction, and nothing was called on the floor. So play ensued as Booker writhed in pain on the court until the Suns were forced to call a timeout.

But there was a chance to rectify the situation from there. Per the NBA rulebook, coaches aren't permitted to challenge a non-call. Suns coach Jordan Ott had no recourse to initiate a replay review.

But game officials do. Under the "Altercation" heading in the NBA rulebook, officials may initiate a replay review absent a foul called on the floor when:

A player, coach, trainer or other bench person commits a hostile act against another player, referee, coach, trainer, bench person or spectator

This is generally applied when there's a two-way altercation. If Booker had kicked Johnson in the groin in response, they would have almost certainly gone to the tape to issue Johnson his deserved flagrant foul that really warranted Flagrant 2 with an ejection.

But the language clearly gives officials leeway to go to the monitor when a player "commits a hostile act against another player." A groin kick is the definition of a hostile act. NBA referees are more than happy to stop play to go to the monitor for lesser infractions.

The only solace here is that the game was a blowout. The Spurs held a 15-point lead at the time in a game they won, 122-96. A properly called flagrant foul wasn't likely to shift the outcome of the game.

But that's likely of little comfort to Booker. Game officials need to do better.

#2862. Credit card debt climbs to a record $1.29 trillion, New York Fed research finds

Americans are increasingly turning to credit cards as financial pressures mount, new reports show.

Collectively, credit card balances climbed by $22 billion in the third quarter to a record $1.29 trillion, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of New York's latest household debt report, released Tuesday. Balances are up 4.2% from a year earlier.

Total household debt, including mortgages, auto loans and student loans, also expanded by $334 billion for the July-to-September period to a record $19.1 trillion.

"Overall debt has been growing," the New York Fed researchers said on a press call Tuesday. "This does reflect financial hardship out there."

The average credit card balance per consumer now stands at $6,678, up 2.4% year over year, a separate quarterly credit industry insights report from TransUnion found. The number of people with credit cards rose 2% to 178 million from 174.8 million.

Consumers often turn to credit cards in part because they are more accessible than other types of loans — even though the average interest rate on a new credit card offer is about 24%, according to data from LendingTree. Credit card APRs vary depending on the type of card, the issuer and the borrower's creditworthiness.

"Credit cards continue to be one of the most expensive ways to borrow," said Charlie Wise, TransUnion's senior vice president of global research and consulting.

Over time, an increasing share of new credit cards has gone to borrowers with lower incomes and credit scores, and "those accounts are now showing higher levels of distress," the New York Fed researchers said in a corresponding blog post.

Credit card issuers loosened their lending standards during the Covid pandemic, and delinquency rates rose in the years that followed, the New York Fed researchers said on the press call.

"Macroeconomic headwinds — namely high inflation and rising borrowing costs — have squeezed consumers, with inflation outpacing wage growth for low-income brackets," according to the New York Fed.

Meanwhile, the cost of living continues to climb, with Americans paying more for essentials such as groceries and gas.

Stubborn inflation has now stretched on for years, putting many households in an affordability squeeze. Costs for goods and services rose more than expected again in October, the Bureau of Labor Statistics also reported Tuesday.

"Higher prices and higher rates have pushed more people into debt," said Ted Rossman, principal consumer finance analyst at Money Management International, a nonprofit credit counseling agency.

Balances have continued to swell in the wake of the pandemic, and even though the growth rate is now slowing, "from a household finance standpoint, a lot of damage has been done," Rossman said.

The reports come on the heels of the Democrats' strong midterm results, when many voters expressed their dissatisfaction with the U.S. economy, according to Kyle Moore, chief economist at the nonpartisan think tank The Century Foundation.

"The throughline connecting so many of Tuesday's outcomes was a recognition that the American economy is failing American families," Moore said, referring to Election Day.

Those adjustments have been intentional, mixing blitzes, simulated pressure, two-deep and three-deep looks, man coverage, and zone. The players have applied the changes effectively, even on days when opponents have moved the ball.

"I think he's done a great job with that. I mean, to me that's one of the important things in coaching, a mark of a great coach, is the ability to adapt to your personnel, your circumstances, the opponent, all those things, and have a system built that's able to do that," Harbaugh said. His 90.0 overall PFF grade leads all qualified interior defenders this season despite signing for a relatively pedestrian contract in the offseason. Believe it or not, Reader is the only player at the position with an 80.0-plus PFF run-defense and pass-rush grade.

#2860. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for steady income

Stock markets remain volatile as investors weigh uncertainty in the Middle East, rising interest rates and concerns about the artificial intelligence (AI) trade.

Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking a stable income stream can enhance their portfolios with the addition of dividend stocks. In this regard, tracking recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks that are backed by strong financials.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is the first dividend pick. Earlier this month, the company paid a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share. At an annualized dividend of $7.12 per share, CVX offers a dividend yield of about 3.5%.

After a meeting with senior leadership, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on CVX and raised his price target to $240 from $225. Among the key takeaways Mehta emphasized was that Chevron is building a solid pipeline of promising international exploration projects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa and the Eastern Mediterranean. In particular, he mentioned the focus on Venezuela, where Chevron expects to more than double gross production to 600 kbd from about 280 kbd by 2031, using three joint ventures.

Mehta also noted Chevron's efforts to deploy new technologies, especially in shale and tight-oil operations. CVX is focused on boosting productivity and efficiency to maximize free cash flow, he added.

Initiatives such as artificial lift optimization, AI and machine learning, and advanced chemical treatments are expected to improve recovery by about 10% in new wells. Consequently, capital spending per barrel in U.S. shale is projected to fall 25% in 2026, with total Permian spending dropping below $3.5 billion.

Mehta also said that Chevron sees power as a growth opportunity and is using its U.S. natural gas portfolio, key equipment and partnerships to meet rising demand. Notably, Chevron recently signed a 20-year power purchase deal with Microsoft (MSFT) to supply 2.67 GW of behind-the-meter capacity. This project, called Kilby, is expected to see its first power delivery in 2028 and generate mid-teens returns and long-term cash flows.

Mehta ranks No. 536 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 11.3%. See Chevron Statistics on TipRanks.

Enterprise Products Partners (EPD) is a master limited partnership (MLP) that provides midstream energy services to producers and consumers of natural gas, natural gas liquids (NGLs), crude oil, refined products and petrochemicals. With a quarterly cash distribution of 56 cents per common unit ($2.24 per common unit annualized), EPD offers a yield of about 6%.

Following a recent call with the company, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on EPD with a price target of $42. The analyst slightly lowered her estimates for the second half of 2026, citing margin and volume normalization, though demand dynamics remain strong.

While the 5-star analyst expects a sequential decline due to normal seasonality and normalization following high spreads in the first half of the year, she still expects EPD to report another solid quarter (Q3 2026) and enter 2027 with a favorable setup. Ahead of Q3 earnings, Scotto expects investors to focus on export demand durability, EPD's ability to profit from spot cargo sales and growth in Permian volumes.

Scotto continues to expect $150 million of common unit buybacks per quarter in the second half of 2026. She expects buybacks to increase to $200 million per quarter in 2027, as capex spending declines and free cash flow improves.

In total, Scotto is bullish on EPD and views it as a core MLP holding that has both offensive and defensive characteristics. The analyst praised EPD's diverse asset base as ensuring stable cash flows. "Furthermore, the partnership's multi-year organic growth backlog helps provide visibility on long-term distribution growth," Scotto said.

Scotto ranks No. 199 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 15.9%. See Enterprise Products Partners Financials on TipRanks.

Brookfield Infrastructure Partners (BIP) operates a diversified portfolio of high-quality utilities, transport, midstream and data assets. The company declared a quarterly distribution of 45.5 cents per unit, payable on September 29. At an annualized distribution of $1.82, BIP offers a yield of 5.2%.

Recently, BMO Capital analyst Devin Dodge reaffirmed a buy rating on Brookfield Infrastructure with a price target of $47. The analyst cited improved visibility into BIP's growth and views its corporate simplification as a potential catalyst.

"Layering in the benefits from the proposed corporate simplification, a multiple well below historical levels and an attractive yield, we believe BIP offers a compelling risk/reward and it remains a preferred idea," said Dodge.

With more organic growth projects in the pipeline and new partnership investment opportunities, Dodge believes that BIP has clearer visibility into where it will deploy capital. That should support continued double-digit growth in funds from operations (FFO) per unit.

Dodge said the semiconductor foundries that BIP is developing with Intel (INTC) are expected to be fully commissioned by the end of 2026, with returns improving through Q4 2026. Dodge expects the joint venture to boost FFO by 300-400 basis points.

Dodge ranks No. 476 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 68% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.5%. See Brookfield Infrastructure Partners KPIs on TipRanks.

#2859. 'Little star' Asterisk Talley not signing with Stanford on signing day. Here's why

Asterisk Talley is going to wait a bit longer to decide whether she'll play college golf.

Talley, the 17-year-old phenom from Chowchilla, California, will not sign an athlete grant-in-aid agreement Wednesday with Stanford, where she has been committed for more than a year. Talley has been arguably the best amateur in the women's game the last two-and-a-half years, putting together an impressive list of accomplishments as a junior golfer. However, with 15 points in the LPGA's Elite Amateur Pathway program, needing 20 for an LPGA card, Talley has decided to hold off on signing with Stanford – for now.

"It's still definitely in conversation," Talley told Golf Channel. "It's definitely an undecided still, but I think it's something to think about over the next few months, and it's definitely going to be an interesting path to kind of take. Both have their upsides and their cons, too. It's definitely going to be a go-with-the-flow kind of thing and see what happens in these coming months."

The Cardinal still plan to sign Anna Song on National Signing Day, the No. 3 recruit in the Class of 2027. Talley, the 2026 U.S. Women's Amateur champion, is the No. 1 overall prospect.

This year, Talley successfully defended her titles at The Fortinet Stanford Invitational and Hilton Grand Vacations Annika Invitational. Talley also won Junior Invitational at Sage Valley for a second time. A couple weeks later, she finished tied for fourth after an emotional final round that her her hopes dashed with a 7 on the par-3 12th hole in Amen Corner.

That didn't prevent her from bouncing back in a big way. She represented the winning U.S. side at the Curtis Cup in June and then won the U.S. Women's Amateur at The Honors Course in Tennessee, two years after finishing runner-up. It was her second USGA title in addition to her U.S. Women's Amateur Four-Ball victory in 2024. She also was on the Junior Solheim Cup team, and at the U.S. Women's Open at Riviera Country Club, she signed for 5-under 66 on Saturday, posting the best weekend round by an amateur in championship history. Talley went on to finish in a tie for 22nd.

On Monday, Talley was named the AJGA Rolex Player of the Year, her first time winning the title.

Talley has multiple ways to earn the final five points needed to earn an LPGA card. She can do so by moving to first in the World Amateur Golf Ranking (additional two points) or second or third (one point). If she can win the Augusta National Women's Amateur next spring, that's another point. Talley is also exempt into four majors in 2027, and a made cut comes with another point in each of those events. A high finish does, too. She can also earn points at LPGA events; one for a top 40 or two for a top 10.

But as of now, her journey to Stanford is on hold.

#2858. Hegseth impeachment effort torpedoed in House

The House of Representatives effectively torpedoed an effort to impeach Defense Secretary Pete Hegseth with a procedural vote on Tuesday evening.

The House, by a 211-204 margin, approved a measure that will refer the impeachment resolution to a committee.

That will leave little, if any, time for the committee and full House to act on it before Democrats take over the chamber and the Senate with a new majority in early January.

House Republican leadership had sought to kill the effort at impeachment, which was initiated Monday by Rep. Thomas Massie, R-Ky.

The vote came a week after the midterm elections, where Democrats trounced Republicans in the first congressional election since the Iran war broke out in February.

Hegseth had faced eight articles of impeachment.

Massie accused Hegseth of waging war in contravention of Congress, carrying out extrajudicial killings tied to boat strikes in the Caribbean against purported drug smugglers and suppressing free speech by targeting Sen. Mark Kelly, D-Ariz., a military veteran.

Massie is a libertarian who lost his Republican primary in May after clashing with President Donald Trump.

The Kentucky Republican led a bill last year to compel the release of files related to convicted sex offender Jeffrey Epstein.

Shortly after the vote, Trump said: "Democrats' attempt to impeach Secretary of War Pete Hegseth has miserably FAILED!"

"Every single Republican, except Thomas Massie, a major LOSER, voted in favor of Pete," the president said, declaring what he said was an "EXONERATION" of Hegseth.

A Republican forced the vote on Hegseth's impeachment. All Democrats voted against referring the measure to committee.

Defense Department spokesperson Jacob Bliss said earlier in the day that Hegseth "carried out direct, lawful orders from the President to secure the nation." The impeachment effort notably focused on Hegseth, not Trump, who is responsible for launching the various military operations referred to in the articles.

Hegseth, at a press conference at the Pentagon earlier Tuesday, railed against Massie and defended his leadership in the Iran war and other military operations.

"What one disgruntled and defeated congressman is attempting is not oversight, and it's not accountability. It's a political spectacle, a personal manifesto driven by ego and resentment," Hegseth said

"It's a farce, and it's a stunt, and everybody knows it," Hegseth said. "It puts a target on the backs of American warriors by signaling that Congress will undermine their missions in real time."This is not patriotism. This is sabotage."

Trump, in a Truth Social post on Tuesday, said Hegseth "gets an 'A' PLUS, PLUS, PLUS for his defense against a disgruntled, angry, defeated, former Congressman, Thomas Massie, a TOTAL LOSER!"

Trump said Monday that he would stand by Hegseth even if the House impeached him. The president was impeached by the House two times during his first White House term. The Senate acquitted him in both cases.

Republicans hold a slim 218-214 majority in the House.

Some Republicans, notably including Vice President JD Vance, have directly blamed their midterm losses on the Iran war, which continues to see intermittent fighting amid an uneasy stalemate.

Neither of the two Cabinet secretaries impeached by the House has ever been removed by the Senate after a trial in that chamber.

#2857. Jayson Tatum responds to Jaylen Brown snub with runaway Celtics win over 76ers paced by Paul George's 36 points

Jaylen Brown delivered the opening message with a pregame snub Tuesday night. But it was Jayson Tatum, Paul George and the Boston Celtics who got the last word in a runaway 123-101 win over Brown's Philadelphia 76ers.

The game marked the anticipated first meeting between Tatum and Brown since their highly publicized split as Celtics teammates. The pair led Boston to an NBA championship, but are off to a bitter start as rivals after Brown's offseason trade to the 76ers.

Tatum got the best of Brown in the blowout win with 21 points, 6 rebounds, 4 assists and 2 blocks. Brown got into early foul trouble with three first-half fouls and struggled to get much of anything going. He finished with 13 points, 5 rebounds, 2 assists and 4 turnovers.

In the end, it was George who took on the starring role against his former team. George was part of the return from Philadelphia in the deal to acquire Brown from the Celtics. He caught fire and led all scorers with 36 points and five rebounds while shooting 14 of 22 from the field and 6 of 11 from 3.

The tension was on display from the opening tip as Brown openly snubbed Tatum in front of 20,000-plus fans in Philadelphia and a national TV audience. Brown said prior to Tuesday's highly anticipated 76ers-Celtics game that he didn't intend to speak with Tatum.

As players took the court before tipoff, Tatum approached his former Celtics teammate in an effort to dap Brown up. Brown, who was busy spinning the basketball, acted like Tatum didn't exist.

Tatum then moved on to Brown's teammate, LeBron James, who was happy to share some pregame love with his rival before getting down to the business of basketball.

It was awkward to say the least and left Tatum standing at midcourt next to Brown like a man who'd just been left hanging.

The ball was then mercifully tipped to break the pregame tension. After the game, Brown told reporters he was simply trying to focus rather than snub Tatum, via ESPN's Kris Rhim:

"I'm just focused on coming out and doing what's best for my team. Everything else was just — to be honest, I wasn't paying attention. But credit to Jayson, he played well tonight. Credit to the Celtics, they executed, shot the hell out of the ball. They were the better team tonight."

When the game action started, it was Tatum who struck the first blow. In their first one-on-one matchup of the game, Brown sized Tatum up from the wing beyond the 3-point arc. Tatum smothered him and poked the ball away off Brown and out of bounds for a turnover.

Tatum went on to score 11 first-quarter points as the Celtics finished the frame with a 30-23 lead.

From there Tatum and Brown spent much of the game guarding each other when they shared the court. But Tatum made his biggest mark on the game with a poster dunk on James in the second half as the 76ers were closing the gap from a 13-point third-quarter deficit.

The 76ers did battle back to tie the game at 76. But hot Celtics 3-point shooting paced by George opened the game up down the stretch as Boston opened its lead to as much as 25 points.

The Celtics shot 50.5% from the field and 44.9% (22 of 45) from 3 while holding the 76ers to 41.8% shooting and a 29.7% clip (11 of 37) from 3.

Tyrese Maxey led the 76ers with 24 points. James tallied 11 points, 8 rebounds and 5 assists while shooting 1 of 6 from 3. Joel Embiid had 16 points and 6 rebounds while shooting 2 of 9 from the field.

The end result was a blowout win for the Celtics (9-2) to overtake the 76ers (9-3) for the best record in the Eastern Conference.

When it was over, Brown shared hugs with several of his former Celtics teammates. But he walked off the court without acknowledging Tatum as Tatum gave a postgame interview to NBC.

Tatum, meanwhile, didn't say Brown's name in his interview while downplaying the meaning of Tuesday's win.

"They all count as one," Tatum said of the game result. "No game is bigger than the other, definitely in the regular season. It's one game. It's one game. The hoopla is definitely over, and we get ready for tomorrow."

Considering the chatter leading up to Tuesday's game, Brown's cold reception to Tatum was expected.

Tatum has downplayed any friction with his former All-NBA teammate.

"There's no animosity, there's no beef," Tatum said previously when talking about his relationship with Brown.

For Brown, who was vocal about his multitude of grievances before the Celtics traded him in the offseason, there's clearly beef. Or lamb chops, as he prefers.

"There's no beef, but there might be a little lamb chops and gravy," Brown previously opined on his relationship with Tatum.

Brown doubled down on Sunday that he doesn't have anything to talk about with Tatum.

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said, per ESPN's Kris Rhim. "Just playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win, so nothing to talk about. Just check the ball up."

Check the ball up, they did — this time in favor of Tatum and the Celtics.

#2856. No Bull thoughts on the ILB room after the Daiyan Henley trade

I've been hammering the Broncos on their reluctance to upgrade the inside linebacker position for years. No serious draft picks spent on it, no trades, and no serious free agent pursuit.

One of the most overlooked reasons that our 2015 defense was so dominant was the quality of talent we had at ILB. We had Danny Trevathan and Brandon Marshall. Both of those guys could cover. Both defended the run. They were playmakers just like everyone else on that defense.

The Broncos signing Daiyan Henley is an A+ move, and I darn near would give them the same grade if they had traded a 3rd-round pick for him.

Henley is a player that has been loved and respected in the Chargers' locker room. He's a captain of the team. He's also a guy who has worn the green dot for the defense (calling the plays for the defense). This is yet another high-character player added to a locker room built on high character and positive culture.

Athletically, he's the most talented linebacker on the inside we have seen in a very long time. His capability at coverage is excellent. He's also a more sure tackler than either of our current starters.

So the question for this team becomes: what happens with our defense?

While I think he's a real option for the future captain of our defense over Alex Singleton, that day will not be soon. He's going to have to learn the defense and be in the system a while before the Broncos can even think of that.

I expect him to be in the mix in nickel downs coming out of the bye. I think he's likely going to take Justin Strnad's spot as the starter before the season is over.

No matter what happens with the snap count, having depth at inside linebacker is a big deal. Having a linebacker who can cover running backs in the flat and tight ends could very well be the missing piece for this defense to take a big step forward.

#2855. Top Wall Street analysts are upbeat about the prospects for these 3 stocks

September ended on a mixed note, with the S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average falling 0.5% and 4.3%, respectively, while the Nasdaq Composite climbed 1.9%. Markets remained on edge with higher oil prices and Treasury yields weighing on investor sentiment and triggering fears of additional interest rate hikes.

In this climate, investors with a long-term horizon can track recommendations from top Wall Street analysts to find stocks with solid growth prospects.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Neocloud company CoreWeave (CRWV) is benefiting from robust AI-driven demand for cloud computing.

Recently, JPMorgan analyst Samik Chatterjee upgraded CoreWeave to buy from hold, raising his price target to $125 from $120. The analyst cited favorable pricing, driven by solid demand, for the upgrade.

Chatterjee said CoreWeave is now more willing to sign premium priced, shorter-term contracts, which lift his revenue and margin estimates. CRWV stock performance has been underwhelming as investors worry about high capital spending, the analyst noted. That said, he believes that higher prices and increased margins will more than offset the rise in debt to fund capital spending, improving the stock's valuation.

Chatterjee noted the 25% price hike in July across CoreWeave's product offerings and frequent price increases by rivals like Nebius (NBIS), adding that some peers are charging nearly three times more for short-term compute contracts than what CoreWeave charges on longer-term deals.

"CoreWeave's leverage of the opportunity [is] evident in its recent press release highlighting contracts signed in F3Q at ~$40M/MW," said Chatterjee. JPMorgan said higher pricing is already contributing 5-10 percentage points to contract margins versus earlier deals.

Chatterjee ranks No. 4 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 75% of the time, delivering an average return of 55.3%. See CoreWeave Statistics on TipRanks.

Palo Alto Networks (PANW) is a provider of cybersecurity solutions across network, cloud, security operations, AI and identity. Following a discussion with management, BTIG analyst Gray Powell reiterated a buy rating on PANW and raised his price target to $425 from $404.

After a review of Palo Alto's product-level growth catalysts, Powell sees clear upside to both revenue and next-generation security (NGS) annual recurring revenue (ARR) in fiscal 2027. He expects PANW to deliver pro forma revenue growth of more than 17%, above the consensus estimate of 14.6%, and NGS ARR growth of more than 26% versus the Street's expectation of 24.5%.

Over the past five years, the Palo Alto business mix, has moved to higher-growth product areas and new markets. from what was earlier viewed as a hardware-centric network security company, Powell said. The 5-star analyst estimates that hardware firewalls and attached subscriptions now account for less than 50% of revenue.

Meanwhile, high-growth solutions, including Prisma SASE, software firewalls, Cortex XSIAM, Chronosphere and Idira now represent more than 35% of PANW's revenue. "Investors may not fully realize the growth potential within PANW's platform story," Powell said.

BTIG is optimistic about the prospects of Chronosphere in observability, CyberArk in identity and software firewalls and Prisma AIRS in the network and AI security business.

Powell ranks No. 320 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 61% of the time, delivering an average return of 17.3%. See Palo Alto Networks KPIs on TipRanks.

In a research note on September 30, Rosenblatt analyst Scott Devitt reiterated a buy rating on e-commerce and cloud computing platform Amazon (AMZN), raising his price target to $360 from $335. Fears that agentic commerce poses a major threat to Amazon's advertising business seem "overstated," he said.

Devitt noted that the growing adoption of personal agents and AI shopping assistants has sparked concern about displacement of Amazon's retail media business. But the 5-star analyst remains bullish, given Amazon's ability to adapt to changing consumer behavior, as it's done in the past.

Additionally, Devitt argues that even if an AI agent helps a shopper discover a product, Amazon can still handle the purchase and delivery, keeping much of the transaction value. He highlighted that management believes that existing Amazon shoppers will likely continue to start their shopping journey on the platform because it has better personalization, accurate pricing and inventory information that third-party agents still lack.

Overall, Devitt expects Amazon's retail business to prove a net beneficiary of agentic commerce. Fewer clicks would not hurt the company if the value of each remaining click and overall volumes keep rising, he said. The argument is that even if Amazon loses some sponsored ad revenue over the next few years, it would likely be only a small part of the company's total business and could be offset by growth in other areas.

"Consistent with our AI framework, we continue to view Amazon as one of the most resilient leaders in our coverage, combining owned compute infrastructure with a physical fulfillment moat," said Devitt. His bullish view is supported, Devitt said, by expectations that Amazon Web Services (AWS) will exit the year at a more than 45% growth rate with continued margin improvement.

Devitt ranks No. 765 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 49% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Amazon Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

#2853. Oregon, Michigan to battle for edge in series history in 7th meeting

It's been a long time since the Michigan Wolverines made the trip out west to Autzen Stadium. With the lone exception of one assistant coach, nobody on the current coaching staff was on the sideline for that game, and the players on the team were either babies or weren't even born yet.

But for the Michigan fans who remember that game, this weekend's clash in Eugene against the Oregon Ducks might bring back bad memories.

The Ducks and Wolverines are set to meet for just the seventh time ever this weekend with bragging rights in the all-time series history on the line, splitting the first six matchups. Michigan won the first three matchups in dominating fashion, but since the turn of the century, Oregon has owned the matchup, pulling off a pair of upsets in the process.

As Michigan arrives in Eugene for just the second time ever, the Wolverines are spiraling quickly. A 15-6 loss to Michigan State a week ago saw head coach Kyle Whittingham fire offensive coordinator Jason Beck and change up the offense. Oregon is coming off a massive road win over Ohio State and saw its playoff hopes creep back into reality.

Could the Wolverines play spoiler, or will the Ducks continue their winning streak in the tiebreaker matchup? Let's take a look back at how the matchup has unfolded over time and the history between the Big Ten foes.

Oregon and Michigan met for the first time back in 1948 at The Big House. The Wolverines were one of the top programs in the country at that point, while the Ducks were still an afterthought. Michigan shut out the Ducks, 14-0, which began a tradition of hanging zeros on the scoreboard against the team from Eugene.

Norm Van Brocklin led a potent Oregon passing game into Ann Arbor, but he tossed an interception and couldn't muster any offense, while the Wolverines hit on a pair of big passes to put the game away. Michigan went on to win the national championship for the second year in a row.

The Ducks would return to Ann Arbor again in 1960 and 1973, but suffered similar results. In 1960, Michigan quarterback Dave Glinka tossed a pair of touchdowns en route to a 21-0 win. In 1973, led by Hall of Fame coach Bo Schembechler, the Wolverines blanked the Ducks again, winning 24-0. Michigan forced four turnovers, and Gil Chapman returned a punt 83 yards for a touchdown.

The Wolverines finished undefeated that season, while the Ducks won just two games. That was the last time the two programs met before Oregon started to rise as a threat out west.

Oregon began to make a name for itself in college football at the turn of the century, and as the years went on, the Ducks stacked wins that put them on the map. One of the biggest wins in program history at the time came in 2003, the only time Michigan ever made the trip to Autzen Stadium. Ranked No. 3 in the country at the time, the Wolverines fell behind quickly against the No. 22 Ducks, who led 21-6 at halftime.

Michigan clawed back in the second half, but Oregon's defense stood tall at the end of the game to pull off the 31-27 upset at home, and fans rushed the field. Oregon running back Terrence Whitehead accounted for 177 total yards and two touchdowns, while the dual quarterback system of Jason Fife and Kellen Clemens led an efficient passing attack. Defensive back Steven Moore even returned a punt 61 yards to the house in the first half.

The Ducks shut down Michigan running back Chris Perry, who led the nation in rushing at the time, limiting him to just 26 rushing yards.

Oregon's win in 2007 over the Wolverines might be even more infamous in program history. The Mike Bellotti-led and unranked Ducks went to Ann Arbor as 8-point underdogs against No. 5 Michigan. However, the Ducks left no doubt, crushing the Wolverines 39-7 in The Big House and emerging as a national contender.

Dennis Dixon famously pulled off the "Statue of Liberty" play twice, faking a throw right and handing off to Jonathan Stewart in the backfield, and then later faking the handoff to Stewart and keeping the ball for a score. The crafty play calls by Chip Kelly and Dixon's big day, collecting 368 total yards and four touchdowns, thrust Oregon into the rankings. The Ducks would climb as high as No. 2 that season until Dixon tore his ACL late in the season, creating an all-time "What If?" season for the Ducks.

In 2024, Oregon made yet another trip to Ann Arbor, now as members of the Big Ten with Michigan. The Wolverines were coming off a national championship victory, but were just 5-3 at the time and a shell of their championship form. Dan Lanning's team took full advantage, as Dillon Gabriel threw for nearly 300 yards and added a pair of touchdowns in a dominant 38-17 victory for the top-ranked Ducks.

Jordan James rushed for 117 yards and a touchdown, while Traeshon Holden paced the Ducks through the air, making six catches for 149 yards, and Evan Stewart caught a touchdown. It was the largest crowd ever in attendance for an Oregon football game, as The Big House packed in 110,576 fans, although many filtered out by the end of the third quarter.

This weekend, Michigan will make its first trip to Eugene since the upset in 2003, and the Ducks will look to keep their playoff hopes alive at home. Oregon and Michigan will kick off at 12:30 p.m. PST.

Contact/Follow @Ducks_Wire on X (formerly Twitter) and like our page on Facebook to follow ongoing coverage of Oregon Ducks news, notes and opinions.

This article originally appeared on Ducks Wire: Oregon, Michigan to battle for edge in series history in 7th meeting

#2851. Malik Willis going on IR can cost the Packers a round in the 2027 draft

The NFL's compensatory pick formula is a little complicated, but to boil it down: Players are awarded points for their average salary over the life of the contract that they signed in free agency and receive playing-time bonuses based on the percentage of snaps they play during the regular season. If you lose players worth more points than the ones you sign, you get a draft pick from the NFL (which essentially mints an extra round of the draft, awarding 32 picks from the third to seventh rounds).

That's why the news of Malik Willis, a former Green Bay Packers quarterback, going on injured reserve impacts Green Bay in 2027. Already, Over The Cap has adjusted Willis' compensatory draft pick projection to be a fourth-round pick, rather than the third-round pick he was initially expected to earn for the Packers. Willis was already a borderline third-round case (there are only two players currently set to earn third-round compensatory picks: edge defenders Jaelen Phillips and Trey Hendrickson), but losing at least four games of action is essentially a nail in the coffin for that selection.

So instead of having what was projected to be the 98th pick in the draft for the loss of Willis, the Packers are now projected to receive the 133rd pick, according to Over The Cap.

Former Packers leaving Green Bay haven't had much luck this year. Offensive tackle Rasheed Walker, who was expected to net the Packers at least a fifth-round pick in free agency, overplayed his hand and signed late — leaving him with just a one-year deal. At the moment, the Packers are not expected to be awarded a pick for the loss of Walker. On top of that, receiver Romeo Doubs, who had a chance to earn Green Bay a fourth-round pick, is also on the injured reserve, which likely means that the Packers will only get a fifth-round pick for losing him.

Overall, here's the breakdown of the current outlook for the Packers' compensatory picks in 2027:

Teams are capped at four compensatory draft picks, or Walker (seventh round) would have been in play — had the team not canceled out that selection with the addition of cornerback Benjamin St-Juste in free agency.

As a reminder, the Packers own their original draft picks outside of the first (Micah Parsons trade) and sixth (Darian Kinnard trade) rounds. They will also get the Cowboys' fourth-round pick (Rashan Gary trade) and the Eagles' sixth-round pick (Dontayvion Wicks trade) in 2027, bringing their overall total to 11 picks.

#2850. Hundreds of schools in Italy, UK evacuated over 'hoax' bomb threats

Hundreds of schools across Europe were evacuated on Wednesday after receiving bomb threats, police forces say, in what appeared to be the latest wave of efforts to intimidate populations.

Italy's education minister Giuseppe Valditara told Italian TV that the emailed threats were presumed to be hoaxes, and that the number of schools affected was expected to rise.

Threats, received just before 10 a.m. local time by more than 300 schools, were signed "Mikhail Andropov, leader of the Vilayat of Misoginistan," according to a copy of the message seen by Reuters. The same purported sender also claimed responsibility for bomb threats emailed to newsrooms in Ireland and Bulgaria on Friday, according to Reuters.

Schools in Scotland and Northern Ireland were also evacuated on Wednesday morning after receiving similar threats.

The Police Service of Northern Ireland said they were treated as a hoax, while Police Scotland similarly said there was nothing to suggest the threats posed any credible risk to the public.

"Based on enquiries and information to date, police are treating this email, which originated from outside the UK, as a hoax," Police Scotland said in a statement.

It comes as concerns grow in Europe about so-called hybrid threats and the continent's ability to withstand such threats.

Hybrid warfare combines tactics such as cyberattacks, sabotage and disinformation to destabilize adversaries without triggering open military conflict.

A flurry of events disrupted supermarkets, blood banks and passenger flights last week.

Bomb threats repeatedly affected flights across Europe in what officials and airlines described as "hoax bomb threats" or "false attacks."

It remains unclear who might be behind the latest hybrid attacks, and whether they could be state-backed.

France on Tuesday said a wave of cyberattacks targeting supermarket fridges was carried out by Shahid Kaveh, a cyber unit it said was affiliated with the Iranian Revolutionary Guard. Iran denied involvement, calling accusations "baseless and malicious."

Incidents come against a backdrop of heightened geopolitical tensions and domestic unrest across parts of Europe, and experts told CNBC that civilian infrastructure could become an increasingly attractive target for hostile actors.
