#2822. Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls

Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Still no losing streaks to speak of for the 2026-27 Sixers.

The Sixers earned a 127-121 win Sunday over the Bulls to wrap up a 2-1 road trip. They moved to 9-2 overall and Chicago fell to 1-8.

Every Sixers starter scored in double figures.

Three reached the 20-point mark in Jaylen Brown (29 points), Tyrese Maxey (27 points) and LeBron James (22). Joel Embiid had 18 points and 10 rebounds.

Josh Giddey posted 19 points, 15 assists and seven rebounds for the Bulls.

The Sixers were without Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Bulls' absences included Norman Powell (right shoulder inflammation/soreness) and Zach Collins (respiratory illness).

Next on the Sixers' schedule is a home game Tuesday night vs. the Celtics. Here are observations on their win over Chicago:

Following his 0-for-9 night Friday in the Sixers' loss to the Cavaliers, Embiid did not snap right back into form.

He was well short on his first shot attempt, a wide-open three-pointer. Embiid then failed to score in the post over Jalen Smith.

Embiid got a field goal to go at the 6:42 mark of the first quarter when he tipped in his own miss. However, Smith canned a three on the ensuing possession to give Chicago a 23-16 lead.

James committed two early turnovers, although he did sink a corner three-pointer during a fast-paced first quarter. VJ Edgecombe added two long-range jumpers in the first.

Entering Sunday, the Sixers' minus-9.6 net rating in the first quarter ranked 27th in the NBA. They managed to hold a one-point lead in Chicago after the first.

The Sixers' offensive rebounding was their greatest strength in the first quarter. They poured in 17 second-chance points. Embiid had four offensive boards in the opening frame.

Jabari Walker assumed backup center duties for the second straight game without Bona. He picked up where Embiid left off on the glass, grabbing seven rebounds (three offensive) in a great first stint.

Though he's undersized in the height department for an NBA center at 6-foot-7, Walker has plenty of rebounding tools.

He's listed at a strong 237 pounds, doesn't mind contact whatsoever, hunts the ball, and has a knack for winning contested boards. Walker showed all of that Sunday. Very hard to overstate how well he rebounded.

The Sixers' first-shot offense also had many bright moments in the second quarter.

James got rolling on his way to a 16-point first half, Embiid made a couple of mid-range jumpers, and Maxey hit two free throws put the Sixers up 60-50 late in the second period.

Chicago evened the game at 68-all early in the third quarter on a Matas Buzelis three, but the Bulls couldn't sustain that run.

The Bulls' defense continued to look like one of the worst in the NBA and the Sixers' well-balanced offense kept the scoreboard moving. Brown nailed a turnaround jumper to give his team a double-digit lead. Walker knocked down a top-of-the-key triple to extend the Sixers' advantage to 12 points.

The Sixers had two apparent injury scares in the second half. Brown limped to the sideline with 6:55 to go in the third quarter, but he stayed in the game. Edgecombe headed to the locker room after subbing out at the 9:08 mark of the fourth quarter. He returned to the Sixers' bench and ultimately returned for a final stint.

On the floor, the Sixers couldn't ease to victory. Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson trimmed Chicago's deficit to 116-113 by draining a three. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse called timeout.

The Sixers were efficient enough in the half court down the stretch.

Embiid made a put-back hoop and a pair of free throws. Maxey drilled a side-step three-ball after a wraparound feed from James. The Sixers converted their clutch free throws and secured a winning road trip.

#2821. Jonah Jackson on Luke Goedeke's grievances: "This is football, man"

After Sunday night's game between the Buccaneers and Bears, Tampa Bay tackle Luke Goedeke had some grievances to air about Chicago defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons. Bears guard Jonah Jackson was unpersuaded by Goedeke's complaints about Simmons launching himself at and/or holding Bucs linemen.

"The guys on his team tried to launch themselves at us on every play," Jackson said on 104.3 The Score. "I don't know any different. That's on him how he feels. It is what it is. This is football, man. Especially in the trenches. It gets rough and rowdy. . . . Did you see that big guy, number 50 [Vita Vea], launching at us? . . . It is what it is. We signed up for this."

As to Simmons holding Goedeke, Jackson was equally blunt.

"They all do it. It is what it is. You've got find ways to combat it," he said.

It really is anything goes when it comes to line play, subject to what the officials see and are willing to call. Especially when there are so many uncalled holding penalties on offensive linemen, arguably since defensive ends are so good and the powers-that-be prefer to keep quarterbacks in one piece.

The best, most recent example of that came in Carolina on Sunday, on the play when Broncos linebacker Nik Bonitto forced a fumble by Panthers quarterback Bryce Young to seal the game. Bonitto was being held the entire play. And he still got it done.

That's the message for all offensive and defensive linemen. Do whatever you have to do to get it done. It's only a foul if a flag is thrown.

#2820. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2819. How Democrats could snarl Trump administration stakes in private sector companies

With Republicans in charge of the White House and Congress, President Donald Trump has embedded the federal government in private sector companies' affairs in unprecedented ways. That could change once Trump's Democratic adversaries take over the House and Senate following the blue-wave midterm.

Democrats, planning to root out alleged corruption involving Trump, his family and their business associates, have signaled they will closely scrutinize companies that have curried the president's favor.

Their investigations may extend to Intel and the dozens of other companies that have clinched equity stakes with the government during Trump's second term. Democratic lawmakers in July asked law firm Skadden Arps for details about its involvement in the Intel deal.

The dynamic could affect how Trump approaches relationships with private sector companies, after spending two years involving the government in their affairs through stock agreements or, in the case of U.S. Steel, acquiring a "golden share" when the company was acquired by Nippon Steel.

The golden share, which allows a holder to outvote other shareholders, was negotiated as part of the June 2025 deal. The highly unusual arrangement gives Trump veto power over several key areas of the steelmaker's business, including those related to employee salaries, capital investment and even the company name.

In a unique twist, the company's amended charter specifies that after Trump leaves office, the veto power passes not to his successor, but to the Treasury and Commerce Departments as representatives of the U.S. government.

U.S. Steel CEO David Burritt is scheduled to speak Wednesday with CNBC's Megan Cassella about what's next for U.S. industry at the B20 summit hosted by the U.S. Chamber of Commerce.

The U.S. Steel intervention turned out to be just the beginning of the U.S. government taking stakes in companies, a highly unusual move stateside. A month after acquiring the golden share, the Trump administration agreed to a $400 million stock deal with rare-earths company MP Materials. The month after that, Trump announced the U.S. would take a 10% stake in chipmaker Intel.

By January, one year into Trump's second term as president, the administration had established some form of equity position in 14 companies, according to the CATO Institute's tracker. That number skyrocketed to 41 by November, including nine such announcements in May alone, the tracker shows. The holdings include both publicly traded and privately held companies, and CATO tracks deals that are closed as well as others that have been made public but are still being negotiated.

Following Republicans' decisive defeat in the Nov. 3 midterm election, the federal government has not revealed any new stock positions. But Trump, entering lame-duck status and on the verge of losing political power over Congress, could keep trying to steer his government to forge more equity deals with companies, said Indiana University associate professor Sarah Bauerle Danzman, who researches the intersection of national security and investment policy.

"He might feel he has less to lose," Bauerle Danzman, who is also a senior fellow at the Atlantic Council, told CNBC.

White House spokesman Matthew Middleton told CNBC in a statement, "Through voluntary equity investments in willing companies, the Trump Administration has partnered with the private sector to rebuild America's industrial base and bring critical production back home.""Moving forward, the President is willing to work with anyone to advance solutions that strengthen our economy and put more money back in the pockets of American workers," Middleton said.

But Trump's so-far unimpeded efforts may face sudden resistance once Democrats take majority control of the House and Senate in January.

Democratic lawmakers are openly gearing up to score wins against Trump and his associates as they vow to crack down on alleged rampant government corruption.

Those probes are expected to center on tech giants like Apple, Meta Platforms and Elon Musk's companies, whose executives have spent millions cozying up to the administration. Democrats are also poised to dig into potential conflicts related to the bevy of critical-minerals deals Trump has struck. Lawmakers of both parties are also looking at Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang, the close Trump ally engulfed in scandal after admitting an extramarital affair with a suspected Chinese spy.

The administration's equity interventions could become a target in that mission, said Bauerle Danzman, predicting that Democrats might seek to boost oversight efforts even if they don't try to cancel the existing stock arrangements.

The next Congress could try to ensure the government is taking stakes that are "forwarding genuine national security priorities" and that do not provide "special favors to political and family connections," she said.

"The story there is more about kind of tying these issues to ones of accountability and anti-corruption, as opposed to necessarily cost," Bauerle Danzman said.

It's also not clear how strongly Republicans would try to defend Trump's golden share or his government's equity stakes should they become a Democratic target.

Conservatives traditionally espouse free markets and denounce excessive government meddling in the private sector. Republicans, including Trump, spent much of the midterm campaign warning that Democrats aim to destroy the U.S. capitalist system and impose economy-killing big-government socialism.

Some Republicans have criticized the Trump administration's corporate interventions, but the moves have received little pushback from the president's party overall.

But in the wake of a crushing electoral defeat, some Republicans "might feel emboldened and actually join with the Democrats" in trying to clamp down on Trump, Bauerle Danzman said.

#2818. Texas Tech vs. Oklahoma State prediction, odds, pick for Big 12 clash in Week 11

Texas Tech vs. Oklahoma State prediction, odds, pick for Big 12 clash in Week 11 appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Oklahoma State Cowboys have an opportunity to continue their fairytale run when they host the No. 10 Texas Tech Red Raiders in their Big 12 matchup at Boone Pickens Stadium on Saturday. The Cowboys are 6-3 overall and 4-2 in conference play under first-year head coach Eric Morris, having already improved by five wins from last season. They enter the contest after a dominant 66-28 victory over Kansas State, and will once again look to make the most of the home advantage.

Texas Tech, meanwhile are 8-1 overall and 5-1 in the Big 12. The Red Raiders are firmly in the conference championship race and come into this game after a 53-48 win over West Virginia last weekend. The Cowboys have won their last three games against AP top-10 opponents on home turf, with their offense currently ranking second nationally in total yardage, and third when it comes to scoring.

Oklahoma State is a 3.5-point favorite, via DraftKings Sportsbook, and undoubtedly have the offensive firepower to justify those odds.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Oklahoma State: -3.5 (-105), Moneyline -170

Texas Tech enters Saturday's matchup with several injury concerns, particularly at wide receiver. Coy Eakin is out after breaking his hand against West Virginia while Jalen Jones will miss multiple weeks with a hip injury.

Donte Lee Jr. is out for the season, while Malcolm Simmons, an Auburn transfer, has missed recent games but is expected to be available against Oklahoma State. Chase Campbell is expected to take on a larger role with Eakin sidelined.

Further, Cameron Dickey aggravated an ankle injury against West Virginia but is likely to be available after taking on light training on Sunday. Quinten Joyner, meanwhile, is likely to be a game-time decision.

Meanwhile, Oklahoma State will be without offensive guard Jacob Sexton (leg), defensive lineman Landon Dean and safety Mose Phillips III (shoulder). Linebacker Tate Romney and defensive end Jaleel Johnson remain questionable, while safety Quinton Hammonds has been cleared to play.

Oklahoma State enters the game with a 7-2 record against the spread, compared with Texas Tech's 4-5 mark. The Cowboys have therefore been the more reliable team for bettors this season, although they are likely to be tested against one of the country's top teams.

The Cowboys come into this one with a 16-13 advantage over Texas Tech during the Big 12 era and have won 12 of the last 16 meetings between the programs. Their home record against the Red Raiders is particularly strong as they lead 14-9 in Stillwater and have won nine of the last 11 meetings played at Boone Pickens Stadium.

Oklahoma State's passing attack could dictate the game. The biggest challenge for Texas Tech will be containing Drew Mestemaker, who has emerged as one of the most productive quarterbacks in the country. He has thrown for 2,962 yards and 29 touchdowns against eight interceptions, completing 66.7% of his passes.

The quarterback has considerable support from Caleb Hawkins and Justin Bowick. Hawkins has rushed for 1,081 yards and seven touchdowns on 187 carries, while Bowick leads the receiving group with 790 yards and 10 touchdowns on 44 receptions.

The Cowboys average 339.1 passing yards per game, ranking third nationally, while their rushing attack contributes another 194.4 yards per contest. Texas Tech's defense will need to find a way to disrupt Mestemaker without allowing Hawkins to take control of the game on the ground.

Oklahoma State has the more productive offense, the better ATS record and a strong recent history at Boone Pickens Stadium. Mestemaker's passing production gives the Cowboys a significant advantage against a Texas Tech defense that ranks near the bottom of the FBS in passing yards allowed.

The Red Raiders have enough offensive talent to make this competitive. Williams' rushing production and Castellanos' dual-threat ability could create problems for an Oklahoma State defense that allows 384 yards per game.

Prediction: Oklahoma State 41, Texas Tech 35

Pick against the spread: Oklahoma State -3.5 (-105)

#2817. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2816. Zelenskyy warns Ukraine is 'not real estate' as experts say a Russia peace deal remains elusive

Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy warned Tuesday that his country "was not real estate," as a second and final day of U.S.-brokered peace talks got underway.

Speaking to reporters in Denmark late Tuesday, Zelenskyy said he was "happy" with what he was hearing from the peace talks in Abu Dhabi between Russian and Ukrainian representatives, but said he had been clear with U.S. envoys Steve Witkoff and Jared Kushner that "this is a country of our nation, it is not real estate."

His comments come amid discussions on the future borders of Ukraine and post-war security guarantees, issues where the countries remain far apart.

"I don't have any expectations of major progress towards settlement," Mykola Bielieskov, senior analyst at Ukrainian NGO Come Back Alive, told CNBC Tuesday.

Bielieskov would welcome another round of exchanges of prisoners of war, "otherwise there is no room for compromise."

Following Tuesday's talks, the first since February, Kushner said he was "encouraged by the constructive discussions."

"We are working to end this devastating war and establish the security arrangements necessary for a sovereign, secure, and prosperous Ukraine and lasting peace across Europe," he wrote on X.

Opening the meeting, Kushner said he had almost daily conversations with both sides and had narrowed the gaps between them. "I think we have some good ideas on how to bridge it."

According to Bielieskov, Ukraine could accept a ceasefire along the current front lines, but would never formally recognize Russian sovereignty over occupied territory. He argued that Zelenskyy has neither the legal authority nor public support to surrender Ukrainian land, even if Kyiv acknowledges it cannot reclaim those territories militarily in the near term.

Russia's demands for additional territory, restrictions on NATO membership and limits on Ukraine's armed forces remain fundamental obstacles to a deal, he added.

Maximilian Hess at political risk firm Enmetena Advisory told CNBC's Dan Murphy on Wednesday that little has been achieved in the latest round of meetings.

While Zelenskyy has urged leaders to speak directly, Hess said it's unlikely the Ukrainian leader will be willing to meet with Putin without meaningful concessions from Russia, given the risk of domestic backlash.

He argued that Putin could use a potential meeting with Trump and Xi Jinping in China to seek further U.S. sanctions relief, weaken Washington's support for Ukraine, and deepen divisions within the West.

#2815. 5 NFL trade deadline moves we wish happened, including Jonathan Taylor

The 2026 NFL trade deadline has officially passed, and it was one of the most exciting in recent memory.

The reason for this is simple. For the first time in a long while, a significant quarterback trade happened ahead of the deadline. The Las Vegas Raiders sent their starting quarterback, Kirk Cousins, to the Minnesota Vikings, who surrendered a third-round pick to reacquire the veteran who spent six seasons with the team from 2018-23.

The Cousins deal wasn't the only major move. The Indianapolis Colts also traded a couple of receivers, Keenan Allen and Josh Downs, ahead of the deadline while the Jacksonville Jaguars were aggressive in adding three pieces to their defense to put a bow on the NFL's trade season.

Even so, there were still plenty of trades that didn't end up happening. Most notably, running back Jonathan Taylor – who was subject to many trade rumors in the lead-up to the deadline – remained with the Colts while the Los Angeles Chargers decided to hang on to most of their veterans despite sporting a winless record.

With that in mind, here's a look at five deals we would have liked to see that didn't ultimately happen as the dust settles on the 2026 NFL trade deadline.

NFL TRADE GRADES: Which team won every major deadline deal?

There's little doubt the Vikings made the most impactful deadline deal by acquiring Cousins from the Las Vegas Raiders to address their quarterback woes. Still, Minnesota could have taken it a step further and tried to pry Taylor away from the Colts.

The Vikings' rushing offense has ranked 27th in EPA per play this season, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats. Aaron Jones and Jordan Mason have formed a solid but unspectacular duo at the position, and adding a true three-down playmaker could have made an even bigger difference for Minnesota's offense.

Taylor would have been an excellent fit, and bringing him in to play alongside Cousins could have given Minnesota's offense an even bigger shot in the arm. Getting that unit to play at an above-average level could be all it takes to turn the Vikings, who sport an elite defense under Brian Flores, into a true Super Bowl contender.

As such, it would have been fun to see the Vikings try to make an all-in move to get Taylor. But at the same time, it isn't shocking to see first-year general manager Nolan Teasley take a more measured approach – especially given the Colts' high asking price for Taylor – while treating Cousins as the team's marquee acquisition.

The Jaguars opted to keep Thomas ahead of the 2026 NFL trade deadline. That isn't a major surprise considering Jacksonville was a big-time buyer, swinging deals for a trio of defensive players – safety Jessie Bates III along with defensive tackles Jordan Davis and Davon Godchaux – to bolster their chances of making a Super Bowl run.

Even so, Thomas has struggled to make much of an impact in Liam Coen's offense, posting just 10 catches for 117 yards and two touchdowns across eight games this season. The 2024 first-round pick could use a change of scenery, and landing with a Rams team in desperate need of help at the position could've proven to be a nice fit.

The Rams are currently without both Puka Nacua and Davante Adams, who are each on IR, so Matthew Stafford is going to have to make use of a receiving corps whose top options are Konata Mumpfield and Tutu Atwell. Thomas would have been a big-time upgrade to that group and could have completed a formidable receiving trio along with Nacua and Adams in Sean McVay's offense.

The Patriots are currently dealing with key injuries at running back. Rhamondre Stevenson is dealing with a knee injury that is expected to sideline him short-term, while TreVeyon Henderson is still dealing with the aftermath of an "unusual medical emergency" that briefly hospitalized him.

Because of those issues, New England is left with only Corey Kiner and Lan Larison among the healthy running backs on its 53-man roster. Kiner has flashed in limited action, but it may have been wise for the Patriots to add another proven running back to the rotation as they look to make a playoff push.

Pollard is on pace for a fifth consecutive season with at least 1,000 rushing yards, and he ran for 246 yards and five touchdowns in his two games leading up to the 2026 NFL trade deadline. The 29-year-old veteran would have been a perfect option as a steady, plug-and-play fit for the Patriots who could help take pressure off Drake Maye.

The 49ers were active ahead of the trade deadline, acquiring defensive tackle DeForest Buckner and wide receiver Keenan Allen from the Colts, but they still could have used additional help for their pass rush.

San Francisco ranks 30th league-wide in pressure rate, per the NFL's Next Gen Stats, and has only produced 15 total sacks this season. Just one team with a winning record, the Kansas City Chiefs, has fewer sacks than the 49ers thus far this year.

The 49ers could certainly see an uptick in production with Nick Bosa healthy and Buckner now in the fold. Still, adding a proven veteran like Mack to help them chase a title could also have been a good play. The 35-year-old has played 69.3% of the Chargers' defensive snaps this season while generating three sacks and 19 pressures. Placing him across from Bosa could have added a welcome layer of depth and talent to San Francisco's pass rush.

And hey, it also would have been nice to see Mack, a pending free agent, get a chance to play with a contender instead of languishing through a lost season with the 0-8 Chargers.

The Chiefs rank middle-of-the-pack league-wide in pressure rate, but they have struggled to turn those pressures into sacks. Kansas City has just 14 sacks in eight games and endured a three-week stretch without recording a single sack.

The Chiefs have performed better of late, racking up 10 sacks over their last four games. Still, adding a player like Sweat, who had a career-high 12 sacks last season and has posted three this season despite playing for a struggling Cardinals defense, could have improved their baseline production and given more assistance to George Karlaftis and Chris Jones.

Instead, the Chiefs focused on grabbing a "Josh" for their offense: Colts wide receiver Josh Downs. The move was sensible given the team's injury issues at the position and Xavier Worthy's inconsistent performance, but it still would have been nice to see Kansas City try to upgrade the defensive side of the ball as well.

#2814. Nvidia shares rebound 4% as Huang faces scrutiny ahead of earnings

Nvidia's stock rose premarket on Wednesday, after falling for two days amid the scandal that has rocked the world's most valuable tech company.

Shares had closed down 5.47% on Tuesday as more details were revealed about the impact of CEO Jensen Huang's affair with an alleged Chinese spy. They fell 4.3% Monday.

Shares were 3.8% higher in premarket trading as of 8:37 a.m. ET.

On Tuesday, Nvidia announced it had hired law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to investigate whether company secrets or national security were compromised.

Huang tried to project "business as usual" as he returned to the office on Monday, people familiar with the matter told CNBC. The CEO was also in the office on Tuesday, conducting meetings, ahead of the company's quarterly earnings on Nov. 17.

Investors are assessing whether the matter will have damaging spillover effects on the business.

In a Tuesday note, Bank of America said it saw three scenarios with differing investment outcomes. In one scenario, the impact is contained with leadership continuity, and in another, prolonged uncertainty pressures valuation before earnings.

In the final scenario, the impact is escalated, with "adverse findings, leadership disruption or tighter restrictions requiring earnings reassessment," BofA research analyst Vivek Arya said in the note.

Zacks' Chief Equity Strategist John Blank said the selloff reflected two concerns: that the U.S. might impose further restrictions in the wake of the affair and that further problems might arise as the news develops. "What else has Jensen done?" he said.

"It's not good for Nvidia. It's not good for Jensen Huang. It's certainly not good for the AI trade," Blank told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe" on Wednesday.

Morgan Stanley on Wednesday removed Nvidia as a top pick after shares dropped more than 25% since it was named on the list on Oct. 2. Huang's leadership is "crucial" to the company's strategic direction, analysts said in a Wednesday note.

— CNBC's Michael Bloom helped contribute to this report.

#2813. Braves predicted to sign $22M All-Star southpaw with elite command to pair with Chris Sale

Braves predicted to sign $22M All-Star southpaw with elite command to pair with Chris Sale originally appeared on The Sporting News. Add The Sporting News as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Atlanta Braves head into what could be a pivotal offseason as they look to defend their NL East title for the first time in several seasons. Atlanta reclaimed the division after briefly taking a backseat to its rival, the Philadelphia Phillies.

This winter, the Braves already have a clear priority: upgrading their starting rotation. Chris Sale is the only member of the current rotation who appears to be a lock to be there on Opening Day.

"While it wasn't their downfall in the end, the starting rotation has been a constant thorn in the Atlanta Braves' side for multiple seasons now. For 2026, it was no different. As the page turns to 2027, it's to see how it shakes up. There will be at least one guarantee. Chris Sale is the ace. You probably guarantee that Bryce Elder is in the mix, too, along with Grant Holmes. After that, we'll see what happens," SI's Harrison Smajovits wrote.

There are also major concerns that three key starting pitchers could miss the entire 2027 season.

"The Atlanta Braves enter another offseason trying to solve the same puzzle: how to enhance their starting rotation. In 2026, they hoped Spencer Strider, Spencer Schwellenbach and Hurston Waldrep were the cavalry needed to solve the issues. But all three dealt with injuries and can't be counted on to pitch in 2027," The Athletic's Jesus Cano wrote.

With Atlanta in desperate need of another frontline starter, SI's Roman Janeiro believes the Braves should pursue Shota Imanaga.

"The good news, and the reason I think the Braves should take a flyer on the southpaw, is that Atlanta pitching coach Jeremy Hefner proved to be something of a secret weapon for the team in 2026. Hefner took a starting rotation ravaged by injuries and maximized the performance of names that might have otherwise gotten lost in the shuffle, such as Martín Pérez, Grant Holmes and Tyler Mahle, and turned Atlanta into a top-five staff. Not to mention Ray Kerr and his postseason hero moment. All that to say, if there's anyone who can help refine Imanaga into giving up fewer home runs, Hefner would be that guy. And if he could, Imanaga is exactly what the Braves need: an affordable veteran lefty to back up Chris Sale who keeps strikeouts high and walks low," Janeiro wrote.

There is little doubt that adding Imanaga would give Atlanta a formidable pairing of left-handed starters for opposing teams to deal with. He has also demonstrated the ability to take the ball consistently, making him an appealing option for an organization that needs more dependable innings.

Imanaga played under a $22 million contract with the Chicago Cubs in 2026. Joining Atlanta would give him the opportunity to work alongside one of the best left-handed pitchers in baseball in Sale while potentially becoming a key piece of the rotation for years to come.

Simply put, Atlanta needs starting pitchers who can stay healthy and provide consistent production. That has been a challenge for the organization over the last couple of seasons, but Imanaga has demonstrated the ability to remain available throughout his major league career.

The southpaw also brings elite command, making it difficult for hitters to capitalize on mistakes and reach base. Adding another experienced arm to the clubhouse would be valuable, too. With pitching coach Jeremy Hefner already demonstrating his ability to get the most out of Atlanta's rotation, Imanaga could have another level of potential to unlock.

The Braves are expected to be aggressive this offseason, and pursuing Imanaga would be a significant step toward addressing their biggest roster need. He has many of the qualities Atlanta is looking for in a starting pitcher, making this a move that makes plenty of sense.

#2812. Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to ‘a regular game'

Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to 'a regular game' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Paul George's presence back in town was one of the many swirling morning storylines at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

For good reason, former Celtics teammates Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum will draw immense attention Tuesday night. After all, Brown has said there "might be a little lamb chops with some gravy" between the two, if not a full-blown beef.

But George's return to Philadelphia is more than a tiny subplot. Following two seasons in Philadelphia, he was the centerpiece of the Sixers' summer trade package to acquire Brown.

George told reporters before the Celtics' shootaround that it feels like "a regular game."

"Had I been here eight, nine, 10 years, different story," he said. "I was here two years. Obviously, we didn't have the best of luck or had a great, I think, partnership in terms of my ability to stay healthy and on the floor. So that kind of played into a lot of it.

"But obviously, it's a tremendous fan base, a great sports town to play in. So that was appreciated. There's no ill will for me. It'll be fun to just compete. And outside of that, it's a normal game."

The 36-year-old George indeed had trouble staying available for sustained stretches as a Sixer. He dealt with a variety of injuries and served a 25-game suspension last season for violating the NBA's anti-drug policy.

George did play well on both ends of the floor for much of the 2026 playoffs. He helped the Sixers come back to win a seven-game first-round series against the Celtics in historic fashion. The nine-time All-Star also brought a veteran steadiness to the Sixers' locker room and was glad to mentor younger players like VJ Edgecombe.

"Just his routine … even when he got hot, he didn't try to force it," Jabari Walker said Tuesday morning. "His game was smooth and I think that's how he is as a person, just not forcing anything and having a calm energy."

George has certainly enjoyed his early days as a Celtic.

Through nine games, he's averaged 20.4 points, 5.2 rebounds, 4.1 assists and 2.3 steals while shooting 42.5 percent from three-point range. Boston will enter Tuesday night's matchup second in the Eastern Conference standings at 8-2, the Sixers first at 9-2.

"I've been kind of blown away," George said. "I think it exceeded my expectations since being in Boston. You never know how much culture is involved and how much culture changes your outlook on things and the effect it has mentally, physically, just being in an environment … and the culture that Boston presents.

"So I've been thrilled. I've been playing some of my best basketball of late, and just been enjoying grinding it out with these guys out here. It's a fun style of play and I can't talk (enough) about just the thrill, the happiness and the excitement and joy that it has brought being here."

He was asked what sort of reception he expects from Sixers fans.

"Not sure," George said. "In some ways, I helped land them a future piece for them. So with that, not sure how the reception would be."

#2811. CoreWeave stock pops 15% after outlook raise eases AI slowdown concerns

CoreWeave shares jumped 15% in premarket trading Wednesday after the neocloud posted quarterly earnings that beat estimates and raised its guidance for the year.

Neoclouds such as CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN have come under scrutiny this month as investors assessed the sustainability of the AI trade. CoreWeave, in particular, sparked alarm when spreads on its CDS widened past 11%, generally viewed as a distress level, as investors have to pay up for protection against default.

Revenue jumped 168% to $3.7 billion in the third quarter, the company said in a Tuesday statement.

The three months to Sept. 30 marked a "breakout quarter" for CoreWeave, CEO Michael Intrator said, adding that growth was supported by multiple sources of funding, including financing backed by customer contracts and around $9 billion in customer prepayments and deposits.

Shares were 15.3% higher as of 8:30 a.m. ET. Other neclouds were also higher in premarket trading, with Nebius up 13.4%, Applied Digital up 9.8% and Iren up 7.9%.

CoreWeave expects $1.275 billion to $1.375 billion in adjusted operating income on $13 billion to $13.3 billion in revenue. Analysts surveyed by Visible Alpha were looking for $1.07 billion in adjusted operating income, and analysts polled by LSEG had expected $12.86 billion in 2026 revenue.

The neocloud has done well to allay concerns about financing by moving away from unsecured bond financing, Zacks Chief Equity Strategist John Blank told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe."

"It might be the bottom for CoreWeave," Blank said Wednesday. "But the shares are so beaten up, you have to believe at this point there's more value on the upside than the down."

Tune in at 9:50 a.m. ET as CoreWeave CO Michael Intrator joins CNBC TV to discuss the company's earnings. Watch in real time on CNBC+ or the CNBC Pro stream.

#2810. Position grades from the Patriots’ loss to the Packers

The New England Patriots got their proverbial butts handed to them on Sunday afternoon, losing to the Green Bay Packers in a game that very much was a 60-minute struggle for the home team on both sides of the ball. The offense looked like its early-season version by repeatedly shooting itself in the foot and letting opportunities to get back into the game slip through its grasp, while the defense failed to offer much resistance against one of the most explosive offenses in football.

The end result was a 45-27 shellacking that raises significant concerns for the team — in large part because its two best players, quarterback Drake Maye and cornerback Christian Gonzalez, both had forgettable days. The Patriots in their current state are simply not good enough to overcome off days from one let alone both of them.

Naturally, they will have to lead the way when it comes to bouncing back from this one. However, they are not the only ones who need to take a step forward.

Let's take a look at the film and hand out some grades.

Quarterback: Drake Maye is as talented a thrower of the football as there is in the NFL, and this was on display multiple times against the Packers. Not all of them were completed — we will get to that — but Maye looked like an MVP-caliber quarterback on multiple occasions. Unfortunately for him, he also looked like the error-prone player from earlier in the season as well, and it cost the Patriots. His three interceptions were reminiscent of those previous struggles: his first and third picks were the result of bad decisions and improper eye discipline, while his second was the result of an inaccurate throw that was tipped for another turnover. For as good as Maye was at times, those plays overshadow everything else and put New England on the road to defeat. | Grade: D+

Running back: Rhamondre Stevenson once again did what he could and continues to be the Patriots' most reliable player on the offensive side of the ball. Against Green Bay, he had three explosive plays, including a 30-yard touchdown catch on a well-executed wheel route. Unfortunately, as with Drake Maye, his 78-yard performance and 5.3-yard rushing average are partially overshadowed by a turnover as well as some insufficient pass protection. On the whole, however, he had a solid day. Corey Kiner did too, but most of his production came in garbage time. Lan Larison was quiet. | Grade: B-

Wide receiver: Mike Vrabel was complementary of Kyle Williams in training camp, but his impact continues to be limited even when given a prominent role. On Sunday, he continued to be mostly used as a sacrificial X-receiver that teams are not fooled by, and his plays included a drop on what would have been a significant gain, an interception, and two targets with Tommy DeVito at quarterback that resulted in him getting banged up. His stock is trending down, something that cannot be said for DeMario Douglas: the fourth-year man is New England's most consistent pass catcher and his five-catch, 102-yard performance proves that once again. He continues to perform well in space and even though he would have contributed to a fourth interception by stopping too early on one of his routes — that pick was dropped — he had a good day. Mack Hollins and Efton Chism also had some positive moments, even though Hollins and Maye were not on the same page on the first interception. | Grade: C

Tight end: Hunter Henry was on a snap count after practicing only once leading up to the game, and he was used primarily in the passing game. His impact was not profound, but he did make some positive plays — something that also is true for rookie Eli Raridon. The youngster has shown a good rapport with Drake Maye, even though he was the target on the third interception (a play that still falls on the QB for being baited into a throw). Tanner Arkin, on the other hand, had some noticeable struggles including when he picked up the wrong player on a 6-yard loss. The undrafted rookie was waived on Monday. | Grade: C

Offensive line: Will Campbell is the Patriots' best offensive lineman, and it isn't particularly close. He was very good against the Packers, even though he and Ben Brown failed to properly pick up a stunt resulting in a first-quarter sack on a 3rd-and-7. Still, he was the least of New England's problems. The rest of the line was uneven, with Jared Wilson and the replacement guards — Walter Rouse and Matt Pryor — all at the scene for sacks. Obviously, some hiccups are expected from Rouse and Pryor; the former has had his issues whenever on the field this year, while the latter is a solid player but nonetheless a replacement-level option. Morgan Moses was solid, as was Caleb Lomu after he entered the game in place of an injured Campbell. | Grade: C-

Defensive line: The Patriots defense had a bad game, but there were some positive moments up front. Milton Williams was at the point of attack on most of them, and had a solid game that included three quarterback pressures. Cory Durden and Christian Barmore stuffed three runs each, with the latter also picking up a sack. However, Durden opened the hole on Jordan Love's 13-yard touchdown scramble in the first quarter, while Barmore was washed out of his gap quite easily on Green Bay's 2-yard score in the second quarter. The edge players — Dre'Mont Jones, Elijah Ponder, Gabe Jacas — continued to showcase their high motors, but they also were undisciplined at times with Jones and Jacas both penalized for pre-snap infractions on third downs. For a team with a limited margin of error, unforced mistakes like these are back-breakers. | Grade: C+

Linebacker: Much like the defensive line, the Patriots' off-ball linebackers had some good plays, including one pressure and run stuff each from Robert Spillane and Christian Elliss. On the whole, however, their impact was marginal and they failed to help with the team's biggest issues while aligning in stacked boxes on early downs. Elliss also took a bad angle from the second level on Love's touchdown run in the early first quarter, while both he and Spillane fell victim to play-action fakes. | Grade: C

Cornerback: Carlton Davis gave up a 43-yard pass before leaving with an injury, Karton Prunty surrendered a 10-yard touchdown, and Marcus Jones was responsible for a 28-yarder; he also seemed to be at fault for the miscommunication on Matthew Golden's 5-yard touchdown. Their issues, however, paled in comparison to Christian Gonzalez's. Typically New England's best and most reliable defender, he had an uncharacteristically bad performance and saw nine passes for 188 yards and a touchdown completed into his coverage area — including five catches for 106 yards in traditional man coverage. So, what went wrong? A combination of things. The Packers performing at a very high level; some of the defensive calls and structures; insufficient safety positioning; bad luck (i.e. tripping on a 43-yard man-to-man catch); and possibly most worryingly an apparent lack of reactionary and closing speed. The Patriots are asking a lot of Gonzalez and trust him with diverse coverage assignments, but one has to wonder whether or not this slows him down and puts him out of his comfort zone. That doesn't excuse what looked like a lack of urgency at times, including on a missed tackle on a 34-yard run, but his performance a) started snowballing at some point, and b) requires some context the raw numbers simply cannot provide. | Grade: F

Safety: A defense giving up seven explosive passes is reflective of a breakdown that goes beyond just one position group; the entire secondary struggled top to bottom — including the safeties. Kevin Byard was slow to move into his zone on Matthew Golden's 49-yard catch in the first quarter, and Craig Woodson did not provide the necessary inside help when Christian Watson got inside leverage against Christian Gonzalez to beat New England's Cover 4 for a 39-yard touchdown. Woodson's run defense was solid, but pointing that out feels like rearranging the deck chairs on the Titanic. The ship went down nonetheless. | Grade: D–

Special teams: Kick coverage continues to be a problem for the Patriots, including on a 48-yard Bo Melton runback in the second quarter and a 13-yard Coleman Owen punt return in the third. That said, there also was a lot to like, including Andy Borregales making all three of his extra points and Mitch Wishnowsky teaming up with Erick Hunter to pin the Packers at the 2-yard line on a 50-yard punt. | Grade: C

How would you grade the Patriots' performance against the Packers? Do you agree with our assessment? Please head down to the comment section to discuss with the rest of the community.

#2809. NBA Trade Idea Lands Miami Heat An All-Star Guard To Finally Solve Their Point Guard Problem

Following their offseason roster construction efforts, the Miami Heat were viewed by many as the favorites to win the NBA title this season. Coming off their latest 115-109 victory over the Utah Jazz, the Heat have shown what they are capable of. Still, their 5-6 record reveals that there is a long way to go.

By pairing Giannis Antetokounmpo with Bam Adebayo, Miami formed one of the most formidable frontcourt duos in the league. On that note, signing players such as Klay Thompson and Tim Hardaway Jr. was only expected to complement the team's core. Unfortunately, the lack of a reliable point guard has prevented them from reaching their potential.

While Davion Mitchell's growth has been a promising sight, the Heat's title aspirations require a player who is capable of bringing out the best in the roster right away. To some extent, many sources hint at Kyrie Irving as a potential target. However, given Miami's requirements, another player could be a better fit.

Given the need for a true playmaker, one could argue that Los Angeles Clippers star guard Darius Garland would be an ideal fit in the Heat rotation. Thus, to facilitate his move to Miami, we explore a potential trade package that could satisfy both teams.

Los Angeles Clippers Receive: Andrew Wiggins, Nikola Jovic, 2029 first-round pick

The financial feasibility of this deal is largely dependent on the Miami Heat's salary cap situation. As things stand, the Heat are $2.8 million below the first apron. Since they are hard-capped, Miami cannot take on any more salary than they send out.

Thus, to absorb Darius Garland's $42.1 million cap hit, the Heat could send out $46.3 million in salaries by packaging the contracts of Andrew Wiggins ($30.1 million) and Nikola Jovic ($16.2 million). Given that this would help them shed roughly $4.2 million in cap space, the additional flexibility would be viewed favorably.

On the Clippers' side of the deal, taking on more salaries may not seem ideal at this stage. Still, considering that Wiggins' cap hit will drop to $16.5 million next season once his extension kicks in, and that L.A. is already $17.1 million below the tax line, the Clippers may see financial benefit in making this deal as well.

For obvious reasons, moving an All-Star-caliber player like Darius Garland would have a significant impact on each team involved in this trade scenario.

For the Clippers, parting with Garland would mark the end of a very short-term experiment. Despite his abilities as a floor general, L.A.'s 2-8 record hasn't looked very promising. Even if this could be attributed to the team's weakened roster, the circumstances are largely unfavorable for a player like Garland to compete for a title.

In many ways, it may seem more worthwhile for L.A. to shift its focus to the future and build around their rookie, Keaton Wagler. Hence, adding a reliable veteran in Wiggins, a promising forward in Jovic, and a first-round pick seems meaningful.

Wiggins could bring a lot to the table for a rebuilding team. Aside from his defensive upside, his championship experience and overall veteran presence could serve as a stabilizing element on the roster. Given that he would also have a larger offensive role in this system, the forward could see a notable increase in production from his current averages of 8.7 points, 2.8 rebounds, and 1.5 assists per game.

A similar situation could await Nikola Jovic. Given the forward's struggle with earning playing time in Miami's rotation, resulting in reduced averages of 2.4 points and 2.6 rebounds per game this season, a change of scenery could do him well. With the potential to be a dynamic scorer, he could be a phenomenal fit next to a player like Wagler.

For all intents and purposes, the true value for the Clippers in this deal lies in the first-round pick they could acquire. With a depleted reserve of draft capital, adding more assets could help structure their rebuild.

The Heat are a team with immense offensive potential. Bam Adebayo's record-breaking 83-point game last season showcased his potential as a scorer. Meanwhile, Giannis Antetokounmpo's consistent dominance only serves to support this point. Unfortunately, the Heat lack a connecting piece to bring out the best in their players.

In this regard, Darius Garland seems like the perfect fit for Miami.

Aside from his incredible scoring and ball-handling ability, the aspect of Garland's game that truly stands out is his playmaking. Whether it is in pick-and-roll situations, in transition, or in basic half-court sets, Garland is the modern-day variant of the true point guard.

With averages of 20.4 points and 6.9 assists per game this season, Garland has showcased his ability to not only establish himself as a scoring threat but also mobilize his team's offense. By taking the burden of facilitation off Antetokounmpo, the Heat can free up the forward to assert himself on the inside and make plays out of the post.

As mentioned earlier, there are enough reasons to believe that Darius Garland is the ideal fit for the Miami Heat. His playing style and playmaking upside would help Miami capitalize on the talent at its disposal. However, the downsides are also worth noting.

At 6'1″, Garland isn't the most physically intimidating player. While this isn't always a factor with point guards, especially crafty ones who can use their skill on offense, it becomes more prominent on defense.

Garland's offense may be promising, but given his defensive rating of 123 this season, the defensive trade-off could be a point of concern for the Heat.

Other than his defense, Darius Garland's availability on the trade market may also be in question.

While there is some merit to the idea that the Clippers would want to build around Keaton Wagler, at 26, Garland is still a young player on the verge of entering his prime. It is highly unlikely that the Clippers would consider parting with such a talented piece without fully understanding what he can achieve with this current unit.

Overall, the defensive issues may be enough of a reason for Miami to overlook Garland. However, if they wish to prioritize scoring, trying to acquire Garland at the trade deadline could be a sensible move.

#2808. Fed's Goolsbee calls inflation report 'borderline disturbing' as central bank weighs more hikes

Chicago Federal Reserve President Austan Goolsbee called the latest inflation data "borderline disturbing."

"It's another distressing report — borderline disturbing," Goolsbee said in an interview with PBS Tuesday.

"We have got a real inflation issue on our hands that has been developing," Goolsbee said, according to a transcript of his remarks. "We're going to have to deal with that."

The consumer price index rose 0.4% on a seasonally adjusted basis in October, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics' Tuesday release. That was higher than economists had predicted.

Goolsbee said he had not seen drivers of prices like tariffs fade over time like he hoped. He also said continuously high inflation on services concerned him.

And the Chicago Fed chief said lofty prices are a top issue for many people he meets.

"It's on everyone's mind," Goolsbee said. "It's on business mind. It's on nonprofits' minds. It's on ordinary consumers' mind."

The inflation report landed at a critical juncture for the Federal Reserve, as it considers its next interest-rate move — and the moves it may have to make beyond that if the numbers continue to come in hot.

Goolsbee's comments came on the heels of remarks from other Fed officials Tuesday who said the rate-setting Federal Open Market Committee may need to act more decisively to rein in inflation.

When it raises or lowers rates, the Fed typically moves them by a quarter-percentage point. But Fed Governor Christopher Waller said in London early Tuesday that a hot inflation report could push him toward advocating for an outsized half-point increase in December, and specifically said such a hike should be on the table if core CPI was "around 0.3." Hours later, that's exactly what happened.

Dallas Fed President Lorie Logan, speaking Tuesday after the inflation report, said she sees a need for two additional quarter-point increases, and said "it may be possible to move more rapidly this winter than the FOMC has moved so far this fall."

The rate-setting committee raised rates by a quarter point in September before voting to hold them steady in October.

After years of higher-than-preferred price growth, Goolsbee said the possibility of consumers and businesses beginning to expect hot inflation is a "major concern." If that does happen, he said it can make the process of cooling inflation "much more painful."

But Goolsbee didn't specify whether he thought the Fed should hike rates at its December policy gathering as a result of these concerns."I'm not a fan of being very specific and tying our hands at the next meeting or meetings in the future," Goolsbee said.

Waller, Logan and Paulson are voting members of the FOMC this year, which means they will cast votes on whether to raise rates at the committee's final meeting of the year in December. Goolsbee will be a voting member next year.

In the wake of the inflation report and the remarks from the various Fed officials, Fed funds futures are now unanimously expecting a rate hike at the December meeting, according to CME's FedWatch tool.

Now the question is about whether the central bank will raise rates by a quarter or half percentage point. The Fed funds futures currently put the odds of a half-point hike at 15%.

#2807. ‘Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics

'Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown was asked Sunday whether he and Jayson Tatum had spoken since the former teammates addressed their relationship during the preseason.

Does he plan to approach Tatum before Tuesday night's game at Xfinity Mobile Arena?

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said. "We're playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win. So it's nothing to talk about. Let's check the ball up."

After months of public comments, documentaries and questions about how their partnership ended, the basketball will finally take over.

Brown will face Boston for the first time since the Celtics traded him to Philadelphia for Paul George and four future draft assets in July. He spent his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston, played nine alongside Tatum and helped the Celtics win the 2024 championship, earning Finals MVP honors.

Tuesday's 8 p.m. game on NBC will also bring together the top two teams in the Eastern Conference. The Sixers are 9-2. Boston, riding a three-game winning streak, is 8-2 and sits a half-game behind Philadelphia.

"Another good opportunity for our team to be able to show what we're made of," Brown said. "I'm looking forward to it. Big-time matchup, two heavyweights in the East. Let's get it."

Brown has not hidden his appreciation for Boston, even as more has emerged about the deterioration of his relationship with Tatum and the organization.

"I love Boston," he said Sunday. "Boston's a great city. A lot of people I developed relationships with, you know what I mean? But I'm just focused on what's in front of me right now. And that's the most important thing."

He also acknowledged everything that accompanies an abrupt end to a decade with one organization.

"Decisions were made," Brown said. "It's life. It's business. Move on. It's OK. … It's emotions. You've got to pack up and move your stuff. Your family's got to pack up and move. But it's life. It's happened to numerous amounts of people, myself included.

"So I just look forward to being able to build with my team over here, trying to make the best of my situation."

Brown faced the Celtics during the preseason, but Tuesday will be the first meeting that counts. He said he did not know whether that would introduce emotions that were absent during the exhibition schedule.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there," Brown said. "As of now, I've got no emotion toward nothing. So I'm just ready to go."

Tatum was similarly restrained after Boston's 117-97 victory over Milwaukee on Sunday.

"It's another game on the schedule," Tatum said. "It's the Celtics versus the Sixers. In the words of Brad Stevens, they all count as one."

Tatum called the Sixers "an incredible group" with a "great coaching staff," but declined to add to the discussion surrounding his relationship with Brown.

"Drama gets clicks and views," he said. "That's not my thing." CBS Sports reported Tatum's full comments Sunday night.

Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla was even more concise.

"It'll be fun," Mazzulla said. "It'll be a good atmosphere."

Brown is not the only centerpiece of the July deal who will face his former team.

George has settled quickly into Boston's lineup, averaging 20.4 points and supplying the high-volume outside shooting the Celtics have long prioritized. He scored 27 points and made five three-pointers Sunday as Boston beat Milwaukee for its third consecutive victory.

The Celtics have not needed George to duplicate everything Brown provided. He has fit alongside Tatum, Derrick White and Boston's returning core as another willing shooter and versatile defender.

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, six rebounds and 7.8 assists. His playmaking has been especially important for a team that replaced Brown's downhill pressure with more shooting and ball movement.

Boston enters Tuesday ranked third in offensive rating and fourth in defensive rating. It is one of two NBA teams currently inside the top five on both ends of the floor.

The Celtics have also made 15.6 three-pointers per game, the second-highest average in the league. They set an NBA Cup record by hitting 24 against Charlotte on Friday, then made 18 more Sunday in Milwaukee.

That presents a clear challenge for a Sixers defense that has generally performed well but has occasionally surrendered clean perimeter looks after committing extra help toward the ball. Tatum, George, White, Payton Pritchard and Sam Hauser can all punish late rotations.

The Sixers have reached 9-2 without depending upon the same formula every night.

Tyrese Maxey leads them at 26.9 points per game. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds and four assists while shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 39.2 percent from three. Joel Embiid and LeBron James have supplied scoring, passing and defensive flexibility around them.

Brown has repeatedly described the team as unfinished despite its record.

"From our perspective, it's been some growing pains for sure, even though we've been able to win," he said. "The result, we're not so much fixated on the result. It's more so about the process, and we've got to improve our process and our mentality to creating a winning environment."

Boston should provide the clearest early measure of that process.

The Celtics play considerably slower than the Sixers, but their half-court execution has been more efficient. Philadelphia will want to create turnovers and run before Boston can set its defense. The Celtics will attempt to control the pace, generate three-pointers and make the Sixers execute against a defense that has yet to allow an opposing player to score 30 points this season.

There is also the unavoidable Brown matchup. Boston could use Tatum, George and White against him at different points, giving the Celtics several defenders capable of absorbing his drives without immediately sending help.

Nick Nurse expects Brown to bring something extra, even if Brown has publicly downplayed the emotional component.

"It's going to be very different for him," Nurse said. "I think probably a little bit of emotion in there, too, but also I think he's going to be very motivated to play."

Boston's rotation is healthy entering the matchup. Two-way guard Devin Carter is out because of an illness.

Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti remain out for the Sixers. Adem Bona could return after missing Sunday's contest.

The betting market views the matchup as essentially a toss-up. Monday afternoon listings had the spread moving between Boston minus-1.5 and Philadelphia minus-1.5, depending on the sportsbook, with the total at 215.5.

That fits the larger picture. Philadelphia owns the East's best record. Boston has the conference's strongest point differential and has outscored opponents by 10.7 points per game.

The surrounding conversation will center on Brown and Tatum until the opening tip. Brown does not intend to contribute any ceremonial small talk.

"He's on his side," Brown said. "I'm on my side."

#2806. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.
