#2780. George leads Celtics to 123-101 win over 76ers in first battle between Jays

The battle of the Jays made the headlines, but Paul George seized the spotlight in his return to Philly.

The Boston Celtics handily beat the Philadelphia 76ers 123-101 behind George's 36 points, as he rattled off shot after shot against his former team. Boston's depth also put on a show against the Sixers, as 8 players hit at least 1 three-pointer and 3 players (George, Payton Pritchard, and Sam Hauser) hit at least 4.

It was the first of four regular season games between Boston and Philly this year and, other than the 2023 NBA All-Star Game, the first time the Jays have faced each other as opponents. The teams' next matchup won't be until Jan. 21, when they will square off in TD Garden.

Boston's starting squad included Derrick White, Jordan Walsh, Paul George, Jayson Tatum, and Neemias Queta. The team's injury report was clear.

Philly's starters included Tyrese Maxey, VJ Edgecombe, Jaylen Brown, LeBron James, and Joel Embiid. Adem Bona and Dominick Barlow were out with injuries.

George opened up the action for Boston with the team's first 4 points, as he capitalized early on his advantageous matchup against Edgecombe. Less than 3 minutes into the game, we got our first glimpse of the head-to-head matchup between the Jays. Tatum was guarding Brown and forced a turnover with some fast hands, then got an easy dunk and a cutting layup on back-to-back plays.

Brown got his first points of the game on a pair of free throws about 4 minutes in, but Maxey led the way early for the Sixers, with 8 of the team's first 12 points. Nevertheless, the Celtics had an 18-12 lead after 5 minutes and started off shooting a scalding 8-10 from the floor.

White had a nice redemption play about halfway through the quarter, where he got back in position after losing a turnover and drew a charge on Brown, who took the ball hard to the paint. The Jays starred on the next two plays, as Tatum hit a slick step back three-pointer that was followed up with a ferocious dunk by Brown.

Mitchell Robinson also put in some good work early on, battling with Embiid down low and grabbing 3 rebounds (one of which resulted in a scrappy putback) in less than 5 minutes of play time. However, he fouled Embiid on a three-pointer with less than 3 minutes to go in the quarter, which led Head Coach Joe Mazzulla to sub him out for Queta.

Luckily, Embiid hit only 1-3 free throws, and Boston rattled off a pair of layups to go up 30-21 with 26 seconds to go in the quarter, which ended with a missed Payton Pritchard three-pointer at the buzzer. The frame ended with the C's up 30-23.

Both teams came out firing in the second quarter. Almost five minutes into the frame, the Celtics had scored 15 points and the Sixers tallied 11 of their own. Other than a lone Luka Garza bucket, all of Boston's offense came from Pritchard and George, who were red hot to open the quarter. With 7:21 to go, the C's had taken a 45-34 lead.

Both sides cooled off a bit about halfway through the quarter. Maxey kept Philly's offense chugging along, but the Celtics took a 54-40 lead with the help of a few three-pointers (2 from Sam Hauser and 1 from Hugo Gonzalez). George's hot streak continued, as he came out of a timeout, rebounded his own miss for a bucket, then knocked down a free throw to complete the and-1 play. He also hit a three-pointer about a minute later to put the C's up 62-47.

The Sixers scored the last 4 points of the half, leaving the Celtics with a 62-51 lead after two quarters.

At the half, George led the C's in scoring with 17 points on 63.6% shooting from the field. He also had 4 rebounds and 1 assist.

Tatum was Boston's second leading scorer, with 13 points, 4 rebounds and a block.

For the Sixers, Maxey led the way with 12 points, while Embiid had 9 points (entirely from free throws, as he was 0-6 from the field).

Brown had 8 points, 2 rebounds, 2 assists, 2 steals, and 3 turnovers at the half.

Embiid hit his fourth three-pointer of the season (and his first in 7 games) to open the second half, but Tatum came back and hit a three-pointer of his own soon after. Brown also tallied his fourth foul of the game early in the quarter, putting him in foul trouble early. However, it was the Sixers who came out of the half with more of a spark. With the help of a LeBron three-pointer, a ridiculous circus shot by Edgecombe to score over Queta, and a Maxey layup, Philly went on a 7-0 run to cut Boston's lead to 69-63.

Following a Celtics timeout, Queta cut to the rim for an easy bucket on a dime from Tatum. It was Tatum's first assist of the game. Tatum also had what might have been the play of the game soon after, as he blocked an Edgecombe layup and flew down the court for a thunderous poster dunk on LeBron. Nevertheless, the Sixers kept chipping away, and underrated Celtics killer Dean Wade hit a 26-footer to tie the game up at 76 apiece with about 4 minutes left in the quarter.

Following a timeout, the momentum changed hands again. Pritchard and Maxey went back-to-back-to-back in a three-point shooting exchange that Boston got the best of. Hauser hit another from behind the arc soon after to put the C's up 85-79. That wasn't the end of the three-point shooting display, though. After the Celtics forced a shot clock violation, Tatum, Maxey, and Scheierman hit 3 three-pointers in a row.

By the end of the third, the C's had taken a 91-82 lead.

The Celtics' shooting stayed hot as the final frame began. Pritchard started the quarter with a deep step-back three-pointer, then hit on another a couple minutes later to increase Boston's lead to 15 points while both Maxey and Embiid were resting. George joined in on the fun and hit a pullup three-pointer of his own to give the C's a 100-82 lead, the largest of the night.

Philly called a timeout and subbed Maxey and Embiid back into the game, but they still had a hard time breaking through the Celtics' stifling defense. On the other hand, nothing could stop Paul George. He knocked down a sweet turnaround mid-ranger, then cooked his defender for an easy layup to put Boston up 105-82 with about 8 minutes left to go.

Brown hit his first three-pointer of the night following a Boston timeout, but George hit another 2 three-pointers soon after to give the C's a dominant 111-87 lead with less than 6 minutes to go in the game. The Sixers subsequently emptied the bench, all but conceding the loss to Boston. The Celtics depth then finished the job and never let Philly even whiff a potential comeback.

George racked up 36 points on 63.6% shooting, including 6-11 from beyond the arc, 5 rebounds, and 1 assist.

Tatum finished the game with 21 points on 47.1% shooting, 6 rebounds, 4 assists, and 2 blocks.

Pritchard, Hauser, and Hugo Gonzalez each finished in the double-digits scoring-wise, with 18, 12, and 10 points, respectively.

The team finished the game shooting 22-49 from three-point range.

Brown had only 13 points on 44.4% shooting, 5 rebounds, 2 assists, and 3 stocks. He also had 4 turnovers and finished with a +/- of -27 for the night.

Maxey, James, and Embiid had 24, 11, and 16 points, respectively.

The Celtics will play the Toronto Raptors tomorrow at 7 p.m. in Scotiabank Arena. It will be the last game of Boston's four-game road trip.

#2779. Examining the roster ties between Patriots, Lions ahead of Week 10

The coaching staffs range from Terrell Williams and Todd Downing to Mike Kafka and Dan Skipper. But the ties between the New England Patriots and the Detroit Lions do not end on the sidelines or in the booth.

Between the active rosters, practice squads and reserve lists, eight players in the fold for the trip to Germany have been members of both locker rooms during their NFL careers.

Here's a glance through the list of ex-Patriots and ex-Lions ahead of Sunday's 9:30 a.m. ET kickoff at FC Bayern Munich Stadium.

Joshua Dobbs, quarterback — One March after signing, the Patriots released Dobbs with one year left on his contract. The veteran backup checked into four games during his lone season working alongside starter, captain, Pro Bowler and All-Pro Drake Maye. He completed 7-of-10 passes for 65 yards with no touchdowns nor interceptions. Entering the NFL in the fourth round of the 2017 draft at No. 135 overall by way of the Tennessee Volunteers, Dobbs logged previous stops with the Pittsburgh Steelers, Jacksonville Jaguars, Cleveland Browns, Tennessee Titans, Arizona Cardinals, Minnesota Vikings and San Francisco 49ers. The 31-year-old was previously in Detroit, too, and returned during training camp following the retirement of Teddy Bridgewater. The journey has brought 28 games, including 15 starts, while completing 62.8 percent of his passes for 3,346 yards with 17 touchdowns and 15 interceptions. An additional 515 yards and eight scores have been logged as a rusher.

DeAndre Carter, wide receiver — A former FCS All-American out of Sacramento State, Carter made stops with the Baltimore Ravens and Oakland Raiders before signing to head coach Bill Belichick's practice squad as an undrafted rookie in December 2015. He would be released, re-signed and retained on a futures contract at Gillette Stadium en route to catching a touchdown the following preseason. The 33-year-old has since toured double-digit teams around the league and went from the practice squad to the 53-man roster of the Lions in October. Through 114 career games, he stands with 1,336 receiving yards, 99 rushing yards and six touchdowns on offense. A seventh TD arrived on special teams, where Carter's run includes 1,523 yards on punt returns and 3,468 yards on kickoff returns.

K.J. Britt, linebacker — The Lions turned to the Patriots' practice squad to poach Britt at the start of November. The 27-year-old linebacker now resides on the 53-man roster in Detroit after making the initial cut in Foxborough only to be released as a vested veteran and called upon as a standard elevation. Signed to a one-year pact last offseason, Britt's career includes 78 games and 16 starts. Selected in the fifth round of the 2021 NFL draft at No. 176 overall, the Auburn product spent his rookie pact with the Tampa Bay Buccaneers and finished atop the Miami Dolphins with 76.9 percent of the workload on special teams in 2025. He stands with 162 tackles, half a sack, four stops for loss and three passes defensed through 994 snaps on defense and 1,315 snaps on special teams.

Myles Bryant, safety — After four seasons with the Patriots, Bryant's stay reached its end in 2024 as he agreed to terms with the Houston Texans. A brief stop in the AFC North followed before the 28-year-old defensive back moved on to the NFC South as part of Detroit's practice squad in October. Originally arrived in Foxborough as an undrafted rookie out of Washington in 2020, Bryant went on to appear in 55 games for the Patriots, including 17 starts, while recording 200 tackles, two sacks, three forced fumbles, one fumble recovery and four interceptions. He now finds himself 77 games into his run in the league while seeing 2,514 snaps on defense and 515 snaps in the kicking game.

Cory Durden, defensive tackle — Durden has been in New England's starting lineup for all nine games this fall. The nose tackle stands with 22 tackles, the initial 1.5 sacks of his NFL career, as well as a new best with five quarterback hits. He began 2025 on the practice squad in Foxborough before quickly earning a promotion to the 53-man roster, appearing in every game on the way to Super Bowl LX. Entering the league as an undrafted free agent by way of Florida State and North Carolina State in 2023, Durden spent his rookie training camp with the Lions before being waived at the cutdown deadline. Stints with the Los Angeles Rams and New York Giants were also part of his path. The 27-year-old had checked into eight games to notch 13 tackles before becoming a Patriot.

Erick Hunter, linebacker — An undrafted rookie out of Morgan State, Hunter signed his first NFL contract with the Lions in the spring. The stay ended at the league's 53-man reduction in August. After clearing waivers, the 23-year-old made his way to the active roster of the Patriots. Now eight appearances into his stay at Gillette Stadium, Hunter has played 14 downs on defense while cutting his teeth with 132 downs on special teams. The 6-foot-4, 224-pound linebacker has stepped in as an oversized gunner downing punts and sits with 13 combined tackles, including one for loss. As far as the Patriots' kicking game goes, he currently sits tied for the team lead in stops while ranking sixth in terms of snaps played.

Carlton Davis III, cornerback — Davis' Patriots tenure has featured 22 starts in the regular season, plus 96 tackles, including four for loss, and a dozen passes defensed. The 29-year-old cornerback arrived in Foxborough on a three-year deal as an unrestricted free agent in 2025 and has been limited to five appearances this campaign due to injury. Originally entering the NFL in the second round of the 2018 class at No. 63 overall, the Auburn product earned a Super Bowl LV ring while a member of the Tampa Bay Buccaneers. The run ended via trade as Davis headed to Detroit along with a pair of sixth-rounders in exchange for a third-rounder that became wide receiver Jalen McMillan. Davis started 13 contests during his lone year as a Lion, tallying 56 tackles, two interceptions, one forced fumble and two fumble recoveries.

Kindle Vildor, cornerback — The 28-year-old Vildor signed with New England in March and began September on the practice squad after being released as a vested veteran. He went from serving as a standard elevation to earning a 53-man roster spot in October, yet has since returned to the 17-man group. Vildor stands with seven tackles, one stop for loss, one pass deflection and one forced fumble across a handful of appearances for the Patriots. Originally entering the league with the Chicago Bears in the fifth round of the 2020 draft at No. 163 overall, the Georgia Southern product spent time with five other organizations before landing in the AFC East. Count the Lions among them from 2024 into 2025. Through 85 career games, including 27 starts, the corner has had a hand in 147 tackles, one sack, two forced fumbles and two interceptions across 2,158 snaps on defense and 605 snaps in the kicking game.

Kirk Cousins to Vikings: Cousins is returning to Minnesota, while the Vikings are sending a 2027 third-round pick to Las Vegas.

#2776. Rapid Recap: Knicks 117, Bucks 85

The Milwaukee Bucks just experienced arguably their ugliest loss of the season, losing to the New York Knicks 117-85 after awful second and fourth quarters. Jaime Jaquez Jr. led all Bucks starters with 16 points, while Kasparas Jakucionis led the bench scoring with 11 points and four assists. Meanwhile, Jalen Brunson led the Knicks with 23 points, while Marquette's own (and newest enemy of the Swifties) Tyler Kolek led New York's bench with 12.

With Tyler Herro sitting out, Ryan Rollins got his first start since Halloween against Cleveland. The Bucks hit the first field goal of the game—a Turner triple from the top of the key—and hung tough in the first half of the quarter, tying it at 13 on the heels of five quick points and two assists from Rollins. The Knicks defense, though, proved stout, with Mikal Bridges rejecting Nate Ament's first attempt on the fast break. After a quick five points put the home side ahead, Jenkins called a timeout. Some positives for the Bucks followed out of the break, with Ament spinning through double coverage for the fancy and-one finish for his first points of the night, ending an 8-0 Knicks run. Kasparas Jakucionis kept the scoreboard ticking over as the Bucks exited the first quarter down 26-21.

The Bucks got off to a slow start in the second quarter, with Kel'el Ware scoring the lone field goal nearly four minutes in. Notably, AJ Green and Ousmane Dieng started alongside Ware and Jaquez Jr., and while it was an interesting experiment, the Bucks simply didn't have the speed to keep up with the Knicks on either end. Unfortunately, the Bucks went on another cold stretch, with a Dieng three being the only made bucket in a long stretch. Finally, Ament finally broke the seal after subbing back in and immediately picking off an Andre Drummond pass to finish in transition with balance and poise, looking more like a seasoned veteran than a rookie. Ament proved to be the lone silver lining during their regularly scheduled dumpster-fire quarter, finishing the half with a team-high seven points and two steals. Bucks enter halftime down 58-34.

Thankfully, Milwaukee got out to a faster start in the third, scoring nine quick points, a run capped off by a Jaquez steal and layup plus the harm. After turning it over on the next few possessions, the Bucks got back on track and went on an electric 15-3 run, forcing a New York timeout with the deficit down to 14. For the rest of the quarter, it seemed like almost everyone chipped in: Rollins, Jaquez, Ament, and Jakucionis all finished in double figures as the Bucks outscored the Knicks 38-27 and cut the deficit to 13, 85-72. This was largely because Milwaukee shot 64% from deep in the third and played a more physical brand of defense.

The visitors kept applying pressure to open the final frame with a quick bucket from Ware off a lob from Jaquez. Unfortunately, that was as close as they got, as the home side kept wearing them down, especially on the glass. Milwaukee scored just five points over four minutes, with the Knicks dominating the Bucks' frontcourt. On the night, Kuzma and Turner combined for eight points, eight rebounds, five assists, two blocks, and three turnovers on 2/11 shooting. At least there was the rare Bogoljub Markovic sighting, and Bogo made a smooth pass to Dieng in transition for an easy lay-up. That was about the only real garbage-time highlight, though, as the Knicks kept pouring it in. After mounting a comeback of sorts, the Bucks went out with a whimper.

59: The number of rebounds the Knicks got, and yes, of course, they won the rebound battle 59-32.

Three Bucks starters combined for just 11 points. One of those starters was Burries, a rookie who will likely take steps forward and backward all season. The other two are Kuzma and Turner, and if the first 10 games were not enough evidence that they need to be benched, then this game should be. The Bucks are starting to lose the identity they built over the first eight games, when they ranked top five in rebounding and second-chance points. Ware played more effectively and put in better effort, so start him and Ament. If the Bucks are going to lose, they may as well do it while developing young players.

#2775. Cowboys news: Dallas adds running back to practice squad

After a career game against the Indianapolis Colts, backup running back Tyler Goodson is expected to miss some time with a knee sprain. Instead of signing someone from their practice squad to the active roster, the Dallas Cowboys looked outside their building for some help.

The trade deadline also came and went without the Cowboys making a move. Let's check in on today's headlines.

Kendre Miller has been circled by Cowboys fans since coming out of the draft a few years ago.

Miller was cut on Saturday by the New Orleans Saints in his fourth season since being drafted by the franchise in the third round of the 2023 draft. He reunites with Cowboys running backs coach Derrick Foster who coached him in New Orleans in 2024.

In his three-year TCU career, Miller rushed for 2,410 yards and 26 touchdowns while also grabbing 29 receptions for 229 yards and one touchdown. In 2022, he led the Horned Frogs with 1,399 rushing yards, and his 7.5 yards per carry as a sophomore in 2021 led the nation. His career rushing total is 14th in program history and the fourth-most for a running back that played just three seasons in Fort Worth.

To make room for Kendre Miller, Dallas released a former starter from their practice squad.

On Tuesday, the Cowboys released DB Reddy Steward from the practice squad.

Steward played in three games for the Cowboys this season, starting in two, after being elevated from the practice squad for Dallas' games against the Baltimore, Houston and Tampa Bay. He tallied 11 total tackles in those three games this season. Steward initially joined the Cowboys in August of 2025, and last season played in all 17 games, starting five.

The Cowboys stayed quiet during a crazy trade deadline.

The Cowboys didn't make any trades before Tuesday's deadline passed. Instead of seeking external improvement, they opted to see what this year's team could do in its remaining 10 games and where it could improve on its own.

"I'm very comfortable in our team," Cowboys head coach Brian Schottenheimer said Monday when asked about potentially making a trade.

After the Philadelphia Eagles traded Jordan Davis away, do the Cowboys have the best DT duo in the division?

While Davis is a very good player who was a key part of a championship defense, he comes with some issues. He doesn't play a plurality of snaps every single game, and his 9.5 sacks in five years show somewhat limited upside as a pass rusher. Williams comes with none of those concerns.

Williams' five sacks in 2026 are right on par with his usual career averages, and he is leading the NFL in tackles for loss with 14 this year. If the Cowboys didn't pull off this trade, they would have likely used one of those picks on an inferior tackle from a very thin 2026 class.

The San Francisco 49ers acquired a wide receiver at the trade deadline the Cowboys just faced.

The six-time Pro Bowl receiver was reportedly traded ahead of the NFL deadline on Tuesday from the Colts to the 49ers, no doubt looking forward to a chance at potentially avenging the loss at Lucas Oil Stadium.

#2774. Man United contacted Didier Deschamps, but French legend wants to join another club

Michael Carrick is still at Carrington waiting for the club's hierarchy to formally communicate that he is no longer Manchester United head coach.

Meanwhile, United have reportedly been looking to replace him since October. Reports had previously suggested that INEOS were not fully convinced by the available coaches, including Antonio Conte, Eddie Howe and Julian Nagelsmann.

United have now decided to hand the job to Mauricio Pochettino, who is set to leave the United States men's national team to take the reins in Manchester. However, it seems he was not the first choice of everyone at the club.

According to broadcaster and Spanish football expert Guillem Balague, Manchester United have been speaking to other managers as part of their due diligence.

Balague revealed that United had one conversation with Didier Deschamps, who possesses an exceptional CV, having won the World Cup in 2018 and the Ligue 1 title with Marseille in 2010. He also guided AS Monaco to the Champions League final in 2004.

The legendary French manager is without a job after leaving Les Bleus in the summer, following another encouraging World Cup campaign.

However, it is understood Deschamps is waiting for Juventus. The 58-year-old shares a special connection with the Old Lady, having played more than 100 matches for them in the 1990s before managing them between 2006 and 2007.

However, Balague assures Pochettino was always "at the forefront of their thoughts."

The same cannot be said about several United players, though. Earlier today, The Peoples Person relayed a report claiming some stars wanted Unai Emery.

The Aston Villa manager has done incredible work in Birmingham and is set to be awarded an extension. United would have to spend a fortune to get him from the Villans at this stage of the season.

Meanwhile, Balague claims compensation for Pochettino and his staff would be around $12 million (£9m).

An agreement in principle is reportedly already in place between the Argentine and the Red Devils.

#2766. Has the Renée Slegers Effect Worn Off at Arsenal Women? – Opinion

Is It Safe to Say That the Initial Renée Slegers Effect Has Now Firmly Worn Off?

Since taking over from Jonas Eidevall in 2024, initially as interim manager, Renée Slegers' appointment was just what Arsenal Women were crying out for. It clearly gave them the uplifting boost they were missing as the team began to click and play like an actual team.

And so, Arsenal Women welcomed their second manager in two years after Slegers was appointed permanently in January 2025.

Upon her arrival, although Arsenal failed to lift the Women's Super League title that season, she did lead the women's team to Champions League glory by beating Barcelona 1-0 in the final and, in doing so, qualified for the first edition of the FIFA Women's Champions Cup, which they won by defeating Corinthians 3-2 in the final a year later.

Clearly, things were looking up for the women, and Slegers seemed to have made Arsenal a force to be reckoned with, especially in the Champions League. However, fast forward to now and Arsenal are sitting in sixth place in the WSL, battling to score goals and stay up rather than fighting for the title, which can now safely be written off for this season at least.

So just what has gone wrong for Arsenal Women this season?

The team has lost confidence, they don't seem to be clicking and they cannot seem to find the back of the net. So far in the WSL, they have scored only 10 goals in nine games, which is in stark contrast to the last few seasons when they were finding the back of the net rather freely, making games exciting, fast and full of goals.

Having scored 62 goals in the WSL in the 2024/2025 season and 53 goals in the 2025/2026 season, this season's decline is rather worrying, with goals eluding them despite dominating games and creating chances. Plus, they are barely scraping by with wins in the league, having won three, drawn four and lost two.

These stats and performances are just not acceptable for an Arsenal team that used to be a force to be reckoned with. Although they haven't won the league in around seven years, they have come close in the last two seasons, finishing 12 points behind winners Chelsea in the 2024/25 season and four points behind winners Manchester City just last season.

But I do believe that by letting key players such as Katie McCabe and Beth Mead go at the end of their contracts in the summer, with both joining rivals Chelsea and Manchester City, Arsenal have seemingly lost some of the attacking flair and backbone of the squad.

Although the squad still boasts talented players, ultimately it seems as though the threat both McCabe and Mead brought has not been replaced, and that can clearly be seen in the lack of goals scored this season.

However, it is not all doom and gloom, as in the Champions League it is a different story. They have scored six goals in three games, conceding just two goals along the way, and have won two of those games, drawing one. Could another Champions League win be on the cards? We can but hope…

Now, I know fans would say give managers a chance, but it is clear to see something is missing in this team, especially in the league. Although there has been some recruiting over the summer, ultimately Arsenal Women are not where they should be, and their struggles to turn chances into goals have been a concern for some time now. Something needs to change.

Have Slegers' tactics been found out, or is it a question of whether she has lost, or is losing, the dressing room?

Whatever it is, Arsenal need to pull their socks up, and quick, before they fall dangerously into being just a mid-table team that is forever chasing the pack, while being faced with the threat of relegation, trying to avoid the drop and being left empty-handed.

Gooners, what do you think is going wrong with the women's team? Has the initial Slegers effect worn off, or do you believe she can turn things around? Can Alessia Russo inspire a Gunners revival?

#2762. Game Recap: Suns bench pours in 81 points in blowout win over Clippers, 133-110

Do you see what happens when you go from Victor Wembanyama to *checks notes* Brook Lopez? (with all due respect to the vet)

The Suns' second unit took this one over early and never gave it back. Phoenix beat the Clippers 133-110 Tuesday night, improving to 4-6. The bench poured in 81 points, Phoenix hit 22 threes to LA's 15, and the Suns led for 44 of the 48 minutes. Seven Suns finished in double figures.

It was a much-needed bounceback win for the Suns. We are now 10 games deep into the NBA season, and Phoenix sits at 4-6 after a brutal schedule to open up. All things considered, it could be worse.

Jordan Goodwin led Phoenix with 18 points in under 14 minutes, going 6-of-7 from the field with all six makes from three. Devin Booker added 17 points on 6-of-14 shooting with 5 assists. Luke Kennard scored 14 on 5-of-7 shooting, and Jalen Green had 13 on 5-of-7, including 3-of-4 from deep, with 5 rebounds. Collin Gillespie scored 12 points, all on threes (4-of-9), and dished out a game-high 8 assists. Man Man finished with 11 points on 4-of-5 shooting, 7 rebounds, and 3 assists. Ryan Dunn added 10 points on 4-of-5 shooting and 8 rebounds, and Oso Ighodaro grabbed 8 rebounds and finished +18.

Darius Garland and Rui Hachimura each scored 13 for Los Angeles. Garland shot 5-of-12 with 6 assists, and Hachimura went 4-of-10 with 3 threes and 5 rebounds. Baba Miller also had 13, all in the fourth quarter, on 4-of-4 shooting. Max Strus added 12 points, and Derrick Jones Jr. and Keaton Wagler each scored 11.

The Suns jumped out to an early 6-2 lead, led by 4 points from Devin Booker.

This quarter was tight for most of the first quarter. Los Angeles took an 18-16 lead behind 8 points from Rui Hachimura, who hit two of his three attempts from deep. Then the Phoenix bench took over.

The Suns closed the period on a 13-0 run. Collin Gillespie went 2-for-2 from three, Ryan Dunn was perfect at the rim, and Jordan Goodwin added a triple.

The Phoenix bench outscored LA's reserves 13-0, and the Clippers' second unit went 0-for-4 from the field.

Man Man didn't attempt a shot, but he still had 3 rebounds and 3 assists in under five minutes. Phoenix won the glass 17-8 and the paint 12-4. Devin Booker led all scorers with 8, though he turned it over three times.

Phoenix led after the first quarter, 29-18.

Jordan Goodwin turned the second quarter into his own shootaround. He went 4-for-4 from three for 12 points in just over six minutes, and Luke Kennard added two more triples off the bench. Phoenix shot 8-of-14 from deep in the quarter.

The Clippers found more offense this time, shooting 50 percent with eight different players scoring. Isaiah Jackson and Max Strus gave LA's second unit some life after it went scoreless in the first. Phoenix still stretched the lead to 17 at 48-31, its biggest of the half.

Devin Booker went 0-for-4 from the field in the quarter, and his only point came at the line. Ryan Dunn did not play in the second half after his strong first quarter. Collin Gillespie drilled a deep three at the buzzer to close out the half.

The Suns opened the second half with a bang.

Miles Bridges finally got going after a scoreless first half. He scored 8 points in the third on 3-of-4 shooting, including 2-of-3 from deep.

Devin Booker played all 12 minutes and found his rhythm with 8 points on 2-of-4 from three. Phoenix hit 7 threes in the quarter, while LA went 4-for-15 from deep.

The Suns did damage on the offensive glass. They scored 16 second-chance points in the quarter, compared to 7 for the Clippers, and the lead hit 32 at 91-59.

Man Man added 7 points on 2-of-2 shooting and a perfect 3-of-3 at the line.

With a 27-point cushion, both benches took over. No Phoenix starter played in the fourth, and the Suns closed with an all-reserve group.

Luke Kennard led the way with 8 points. Jamaree Bouyea made all five of his shots, going 2-of-2 from the field and 3-of-3 at the line for 7 points, and added 3 assists. Koa Peat chipped in 5 points, and Rasheer Fleming knocked down his only three. Phoenix shot 12-of-16 in the quarter.

Baba Miller made the most of his minutes for Los Angeles, scoring 13 points in the fourth on 4-of-4 shooting and 4-of-6 at the line. The Clippers took the quarter 38-34, helped by six Phoenix turnovers.

The Suns' never-ending homestand continues as they take on the Hornets on Thursday night.

The thing about it is, don’t tell the world something that’s not me. I’m [going to] speak up for myself all the time because there’s nobody else that’s going to be able to speak up for me. Like I said, I’ve never been on a team where anyone has called me a bad teammate that I know of.”

Olsen took issue with the incorrect quotes about Nabers being a bad teammate and the wideout was asked if he’s heard from directly from him since Sunday. #2750. Lakers vs. Bucks Preview: Trap game in the making

The streaking Lakers (10-2) continue their current four-game road trip in Milwaukee where they will take on the Bucks (1-10). This is the first matchup between the teams this season.

Who: Los Angeles Lakers vs. Milwaukee Bucks

The Lakers have been playing a lot of basketball lately.

Although the Lakers have been playing like a very good team, their schedule hasn't made it easy. They've played four games in the last seven nights and this East Coast road trip has only added to the challenge. They have a well-deserved three-day break coming up. But first, they're going to face arguably the worst team in the league.

This certainly has all the makings of a trap game.

They should be able to win given how serious they've approached every game ahead of them thus far this season. The theme of this road trip has been their next-man mentality, which has kept their winning streak to six straight games. On Wednesday, it's another chance to keep building.

Los Angeles will take on a Milwaukee team led by Tyler Herro (who is currently listed as day-to-day with an injury), Kyle Kuzma and Jaime Jaquez Jr. Basically, it's a rebuilding Bucks team that moved on to its next chapter without franchise legend Giannis Antetokounmpo. They've been experiencing some growing pains on both ends of the floor, where they rank in the bottom five in the league. While they're a very good rebounding team, the Bucks don't have the firepower to keep up with the Lakers — at least they shouldn't.

The only way this match becomes a threat for the Lakers is if one of their streaky players — like Kuzma, Jaquez Jr. or Ryan Rollins — turns out to be the best player on the floor. That's going to be extremely hard to do with Luka Dončić and Austin Reaves set to play.

Hopefully, it won't take much from Dončić and Reaves to get the Lakers through. It would be nice to see the role players and bench continue to build on the momentum they've gained.

Remember, it's games like this that end up being huge in the standings down the line. These are must-win games for the purple and gold, especially in the competitive Western Conference where they're currently sitting on top of the standings.

Let's see if the Lakers can take care of business and avoid a trap-game stumble on Wednesday.

For the Lakers' injury report, only Quentin Grimes (right finger sprain) is listed as questionable.

As for the Bucks, no injury report has been released since they'll be playing on the second night of a back-to-back. They're coming off a 32-point loss against the New York Knicks.

It's also worth noting that Tyler Herro (right knee contusion) didn't play on Tuesday.

#2743. Celtics rout 76ers in first matchup between Jayson Tatum and Jaylen Brown

Celtics rout 76ers in first matchup between Jayson Tatum and Jaylen Brown originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

Round 1 of the new-look Sixers vs. the new-look Celtics decisively went Boston's way on Tuesday night.

In the first meeting between the teams since the July trade that sent Jaylen Brown to Philadelphia and Paul George to Boston, the Celtics earned a 123-101 win at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

The Sixers dropped to 9-3 and the Celtics improved to 9-2.

George was the game's brightest star, scoring 36 points on 14-for-22 shooting. Jayson Tatum added 21 points.

Tyrese Maxey's 24 points led the Sixers. Brown was hampered by foul trouble in his heavily anticipated first outing against Tatum. He posted 13 points, five rebounds and two assists in 26 minutes.

The Sixers will host the Raptors on Thursday night. Here are observations on their blowout loss to the Celtics:

To no one's surprise, Brown and Tatum guarded each other to open the night.

VJ Edgecombe defended a former teammate in George, who scored Boston's first four points with a pair of pure jumpers. On the other end, Maxey made the Sixers' first three field goals.

Judging by volume, the home fans cared most about the much-scrutinized Brown vs. Tatum matchup. The crowd was excited early when Brown snagged a steal and then dished to Maxey for a fast-break three-pointer. The fans also dialed up the noise when Brown sized up Tatum in isolation. He wound up dribbling the ball off his foot and out of bounds. Tatum pointed to signal it was Celtics ball.

Boston's star forward had a productive first quarter, posting 11 points. He scored an easy two when he used a back screen to break free from Brown and convert a layup that put the Celtics up 11-7. Boston began 9 for 12 from the floor as a team.

As for Brown, he sat shortly after being called for his second foul with 6:48 left in the first quarter.

Brown re-entered at the start of the second quarter. The Sixers still struggled to eat into Boston's lead.

A Sam Hauser triple extended the Celtics' advantage to 12 points. There was some audible frustration in the building after Brown missed two free throws on the next possesion.

The Sixers' deficit hit 16 points on a Hugo Gonzalez jumper. Meanwhile, the Sixers missed 12 of their first 14 three-point tries. While some of those shots were open looks that simply didn't go down, the Sixers also rushed up several contested jumpers before they made Boston's defense do serious work.

In the bigger picture, it wouldn't be remotely surprising if shooting stands out as a weak area for the Sixers come the trade deadline. They entered the game ranked 27th in the NBA in three-point frequency outside of garbage time and 22nd in three-point accuracy, according to Cleaning the Glass.

Joel Embiid (16 points, six rebounds) had an injury scare late in the second quarter when he fell hard after missing a layup in transition. He rose gingerly and limped through the final 30 seconds or so of the first half.

The Sixers' star big man was fine to start the third quarter and even drained a long-overdue three on the opening possession. He'd been in a terrible outside shooting slump (3 for 32 from deep on the season) before that shot.

Brown's foul trouble persisted. He picked up his fourth personal with 10:40 to go in the third quarter.

The Sixers stormed back without him and their defense played a large role. After LeBron James rumbled in for a layup, Dean Wade stonewalled Payton Pritchard on the perimeter to force a shot-clock violation. Wade later canned a three to even the contest at 76-all.

The Celtics responded and Tatum led the charge. His third-quarter exploits included a highlight slam on James and a top-of-the-key three to build Boston's lead back up to nine points.

Pritchard buried a three on the first possession of the fourth quarter and the Celtics' advantage kept growing. George celebrated a triple that stretched his team's lead to 100-82. The Sixers' spirits were considerably lower as they headed to their bench for a timeout. As a Sixer, George only had one higher-scoring game (39 points) than his performance Tuesday.

George and Tatum were each excellent in Philadelphia. Outside of Maxey, who finished 8 for 12 from the field, the Sixers didn't receive similar games from their stars.

Brown will be back in Boston on Jan. 21 when the Sixers visit the Celtics. He'll try to even the score.

#2742. John Spytek: First "real" call on Kirk Cousins came this morning

As NFL trades slip into hibernation until March, it’s important to remember something the next time players can be sent from one team to another.

Teams push plenty of BS to the media — and the media often passes it along without scrutiny.

Case in point: The internally inconsistent claim made by ESPN on Saturday, undoubtedly based on information straight from the Raiders.

“As of this weekend,” Adam Schefter of ESPN tweeted, “the Raiders had not received any trade inquiries regarding their starting quarterback Kirk Cousins, nor are they interested in dealing him, per league sources. They plan to keep sticking with him until they believe their No. 1 overall pick Fernando Mendoza is ready.”

So the Raiders hadn’t received any trade inquiries, but they weren’t interested in the trade inquiries they hadn’t received? Right.

In a press conference with reporters on Tuesday, Raiders G.M. John Spytek addressed how it all came to be.

“I never made one call on Kirk Cousins,” Spytek said. “And I got a few phone calls. But one real one this morning.”

The “real one” ultimately entailed getting a third-round pick for Cousins. Which further highlights how silly it is for a team to claim — and for the media to share — the idea that “WE’RE NOT INTERESTED.”

Everyone is interested in a trade offer for all but a small handful of players in the entire league. Anything else is posturing. And, as is often the case when it comes to NFL reporting, the insiders allow themselves to become extensions of the team’s P.R. department in an effort to send a given message.

The Raiders weren’t interested, until they were. And it didn’t take all that much to make them interested in giving up their starting quarterback.

It was easier to make the decision because they were surely thinking about when to bench him for rookie Fernando Mendoza. And it surely was easier for Cousins to embrace the trade, given that he’s now in a position to keep playing for a team that has a chance to get to the postseason and to make some noise in January.

#2740. Cardinals post-NFL trade deadline moves & workouts

The Arizona Cardinals weren't buyers or sellers at the NFL trade deadline. They simply stood pat, and life – and the schedule – moved along.

It was assumed that at least two Cardinals players would be shipped off to new homes, but that never happened.

RELATED: CARDINALS TURN DOWN TRADE OFFER FOR BUDDA

So, after the trade deadline, there was plenty of activity with the franchise.

The big news is that the Cardinals announced they had placed QB Gardner Minshew on IR. There was some question as to whether Minshew would need to be placed on IR, or if his torn UCL in his throwing arm wasn't as serious as first thought. But it was. The word is that it could be season-ending, but that has not been confirmed.

Arizona also placed WR Michael Wilson officially on IR as expected. He injured his neck on the fourth offensive play during last week's game against the Seattle Seahawks. But get this: He played the entire game. One word: Ironman.

Obviously, both of these injuries are crippling events for the offense. This side of the ball is already without OT Paris Johnson, WRs Jalen Brooks and Kendrick Bourne, OG Chase Bisontis, RBs Jeremiyah Love and James Conner, and TE Tip Reiman.

The only other roster move is that WR Phillip Dorsett was signed to the practice squad.

Dorsett (5'-10", 185 pounds) was a beast in college at Miami, where he was clocked at 4.33 in the 40. As a senior, he led the conference in average yards per catch (24.2) and touchdowns (10). He was drafted 29th overall in the first round of the 2015 NFL Draft by the Indianapolis Colts, but after two seasons, he was traded to the New England Patriots for QB Jacoby Brissett.

Arizona is his 10th NFL club. Now age 33, Dorsett has been signed to six different practice squads. He did not appear in any regular-season NFL games in 2024, 2025, or this year. Obviously, the receiver position is a need for the Cardinals. Dorsett joins WR Shedrick Jackson and WR/KR James Proche on the practice squad.

Center Hjalte Froholdt is on concussion protocol from the Seahawks game. That usually takes 10 days to get clearance, which most likely means he won't be available for the home contest this weekend against the Los Angeles Rams. The depth chart lists Kingsley Eguakun as his backup. He has played in seven NFL games with two starts.

On Tuesday, while the league was swirling with trade news, the Cardinals worked out five players:

At one time, Rush (6'-3", 224 pounds) was considered the heir apparent to the starting quarterback position with the Dallas Cowboys. This is his ninth NFL season, and he has been a member of three NFL clubs. He has played in 44 games with 18 starts, thrown for 3,995 yards with a 60.8% completion ratio, and thrown 21 TDs to 18 picks. He appeared in two games for the Atlanta Falcons this year before being released on October 19.

Siemian (6'-3", 220 pounds) is a former seventh-round selection of the Denver Broncos who eventually became the starter in his second year after Peyton Manning retired. He ended up on IR after Week 15, and then was traded to the Minnesota Vikings for a fifth-round pick, but never played backing up Kirk Cousins. He ended up on three other NFL rosters. He was placed on two practice squads in 2023 and 2024, and hasn't played a single down since the 2023 season.

Meiga (6'-4", 201 pounds) played for Coastal Carolina with a 16.7 average yards per reception and went undrafted in the 2024 NFL Draft. He signed with the Seahawks and then the Carolina Panthers. He is Canadian-born. Meiga has not appeared in any regular-season games.

Pancol (6'-3", 205 pounds) went undrafted in 2025 after a good career at Duke. He signed with the Jacksonville Jaguars and was waived in August with an injury designation as part of final roster cuts. He then signed with the Indianapolis Colts, who placed him on their practice squad but he was not re-signed for 2026. He was placed on the practice squad of the Denver Broncos on October 27, then waived on November 3. He has not appeared in any regular-season games.

Out of the five, Dorsett was the only player signed to a deal. Often, teams bring in guys to update their free agent database in the event a player (or two) becomes injured, but in this case, both the quarterback and receiver positions were recently hit with the injury bug, so talent was brought in for a closer look.

At the movies, sometimes there is a short little blurb right after the credits have ended. Every CG cartoon has one, as do the horror and superhero flicks. So, this is our blurb for those who are still reading.

RB Dameon Pierce was a fourth-round draft pick of the Houston Texans back in 2022 and became a Pro Bowl alternate that same year. His highlight was a 98-yard kickoff return for a touchdown in 2023. After his four-year rookie contract expired in 2025, the Texans decided to keep Nick Chubb instead. Pierce was signed by the Kansas City Chiefs to their practice squad and then was elevated to the main roster in late December.

In free agency this year, he signed a one-year $1.29 million deal with the Philadelphia Eagles, but was a last-day cut and then signed to their practice squad. Before Week 6, Pierce was elevated to the main roster. He suffered a hamstring injury, and Philly released him with an injury settlement.

He is now a free agent. Seven teams have reached out to his agent: San Francisco, Miami, Tennessee, Baltimore, Jacksonville, Carolina, and the Cardinals.

Arizona has three running backs on its active roster: Tyler Allgeier, Bam Knight, and Evan Hull, plus veteran Jerome Ford on the practice squad. They are expecting James Conner to return at some point.

What does this mean for the team to go fishing on Pierce? Knight has had some injury issues, but with the availability of Ford, why the looksy? Does this indicate that the team doesn't have any confidence in Conner's return this season? Hmmm……

#2739. Jaguars-Ravens averages 13.96 million on Prime Video

When it comes to interpreting press releases regarding the audiences for sporting events, there’s a rule of thumb.

The higher the number appears in the release, the better the company thinks the audience is. The lower the number appears in the release, the less impactful the figure is.

And if the number is really bad, there’s no release at all.

For the Week 9 Thursday night game between the Jaguars and Ravens— which was destined to have a non-stellar audience thanks to the Ravens being down to their third-string quarterback — Prime Video led with the fact that the eight-game season-to-date average has outperformed last year’s numbers.

The 33-10 win by the Jaguars, which Prime Video accurately called “lopsided” in the release, generated an average of 13.96 million viewers.

It nevertheless was, as Prime Video notes, a 13-percent bump over last year’s Week 9 Thursday night game. And for good reason. The Week 9 Thursday night game in 2025 featured the Ravens at the Dolphins, a 28-6 Baltimore win.

Even with an audience of less than 14 million in the eighth TNF game of the season, the package is offering 17.15 million per week for 2026.

This week’s game — Commanders at Giants — should do well, given that the Giants are both doing well and occupy the nation’s top market. In Week 11, it’s Colts at Texans. Which won’t be great.

The following week, Prime Video has a potentially massive game on Black Friday between the currently 7-2 Broncos and 6-2 Steelers.

After that, it’s Chiefs-Rams, Vikings-Patriots, 49ers-Chargers, Texans-Eagles (on Christmas Eve), and Ravens-Bengals.

#2737. Jockey O'Brien dies aged 39

Jockey Tom O'Brien has died at the age of 39 after being diagnosed with cancer.

Irishman O'Brien rode more than 1,000 winners in Britain and Ireland, including two at the Cheltenham Festival and the Welsh Grand National twice.

The nephew of trainer Aidan O'Brien, he was a mainstay of the Minehead operation run by Philip Hobbs, joining as a teenager and remaining until his retirement in 2023.

"He touched countless lives on both sides of the Irish Sea, bringing joy to everyone who had the privilege of knowing him and watching what he did best, on and off the racecourse," said British Horseracing Authority (BHA) chief executive Brant Dunshea.

"Tom was held in the highest esteem and meant so much to so many people across our sport, who benefitted from his kindness and loyal friendship.

"He will be hugely missed and his loss is immensely difficult to process.

"The racing family is distraught, but I know how we will all rally round to provide each other with support, comfort and to remember Tom as we knew him best: a brilliant jockey, a valued colleague, a loyal friend and above all a loving husband and devoted father."

Riding the Hobbs-trained Thyme Hill, O'Brien won the Liverpool Hurdle in 2021 and the Kauto Star Novices' Chase in 2022.

"It's very sad, particularly for his wife Hayley and two young children," said Hobbs, who now shares Minehead with Johnson White.

"He came to our yard from Ireland having not ridden in a race, but it soon became obvious he was pretty talented.

"He was always very reliable and a very good bloke. There's lots of good memories and there were lots of winners.

"I'd kept in touch with Tom since his retirement as he only lived half an hour away. He was only in our yard about three weeks ago."

O'Brien joined the BHA stewarding team after ending his racing career.

#2736. Sixers Bell Ringer: Rough night for all as Sixers crushed by barrage of Celtics triples

Bell Ringer Standings:Tyrese Maxey – 3.5Jaylen Brown – 2.5VJ Edgecombe – 2Joel Embiid – 1LeBron James – 1Anfernee Simons – 1

Well, that could have gone better. The Philadelphia 76ers were absolutely buried by a barrage of three-pointers from the Boston Celtics and fell 123-101 on Tuesday night.

It was a highlight anticipated matchup of Eastern Conference teams, with Jaylen Brown facing his former squad for the first time as a member of the Sixers. This also meant the return of Paul George, who now sports Celtics green… and he made himself known in this one, to say the least.

It was a rough first half for the Sixers, who looked completely flat offensively and lacked any foundation on defense. They'd manage a bucket, then immediately allow the Celtics to knock down another three. Looking back, Philadelphia were fortunate to only be down 11 points going into halftime. A minor miracle.

The Sixers came out a much better team in the third, finally knocking down some shots and chipping away at the Celtics lead to bring things back even… for a moment.

Boston stayed red hot from beyond the arc, and rebuilt their lead to nine going into the final frame. And they just didn't stop. Three after three after three. They hit nine threes on a run spanning from late in the third into the fourth. It was relentless. Meanwhile, the Sixers didn't score for nearly five whole minutes to start the fourth. Hard to keep a game close with that kind of math going against you!

The Sixers are off Wednesday night and will return on Thursday evening hosting the Toronto Raptors.

Until then, let's get to the Bell Ringer, if we must.

Tyrese Maxey: 24 points, 1 rebound, 4 assists

(Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images)

Tyrese Maxey got his night going quickly with a 3-for-3 start from the floor for eight quick points in the first. He ended up leading the Sixers after their lackluster first half with 12 points on 4-for-6 field goal, 1-for-1 three-point shooting. It was a rough first half for really every Sixer, but his contributions were part of why they were only down a dozen at the break. And he wasn't done. In the third, things really started to heat up for Maxey, who put up another dozen points in the frame with some silky smooth 4-for-5 field goal shooting to help give the Sixers their only real taste of momentum all game, including knocking down two big threes.

It wasn't nearly enough to survive the Celtics absolutely onslaught from long range, but Maxey was the only Sixer to shoot well throughout the night and was critical to the brief, fleeting moment the Sixers looked like they might turn this one around.

Maxey finished leading the Sixers with 24 points on 8-for-12 field goal, 3-for-4 three-point shooting with a rebound and four assists.

(Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images)

Honestly, Dean Wade was one of the only players I thought put in good minutes throughout the entire first half, rather than just a moment here or there. Wade put up some incredible defense on Jayson Tatum at times in the first frame, was grabbing boards left and right and even knocked down a few buckets. At the half, he actually led the Sixers in +/- as a 0. Not bad for a game they trailed by 11 at the time. He got right back to work in the third, putting up more some extremely-respectable defense and adding a triple that briefly tied the game up.

Wade has done this a few games now where he really just does all the little fundamental things right. Even if it's just going up for every defensive board he can get his hands on, or just putting up solid defense that never really shows on basic stat sheet, to simply knocking in a few points when the opportunity presents itself. Wade has been an absolute asset for the Sixers off the bench and he deserves some recognition for it.

Wade finished with eight points and nine rebounds (two offensive). And that's part of what I mean, it hardly sounds like a crazy stat line, but he was truly one of the more consistently productive players for Philadelphia all night.

VJ Edgecombe: 8 points, 4 rebounds, 5 assists, 1 steal, 1 block

(Photo by Jesse D. Garrabrant/NBAE via Getty Images)

Of the rough first half performances from the Sixers, VJ Edgecombe put up one the of the least-rough. Thinking positive, people! The sophomore filled the stat sheet with six points, three rebounds, two assists, one steal and one block by the break. Edgecombe came out to start the second half and hit a truly insane finish through contact from Neemias Queta. It was part of the only little run the Sixers could really string together tonight in the third frame.

The bar was pretty low tonight, so I honestly was giving a nomination to VJ regardless just to show off this play. It was one of the very few exciting moments of the night for Philadelphia. Edgecombe finished with eight points, four rebounds, five assists, a steal and a block.

#2735. In Kirk Cousins, the Vikings landed their first choice

After last night’s abysmal performance by the offense against the Bills, the Vikings mobilized to trade for a quarterback. Ultimately, they got the guy they wanted.

Per a source with knowledge of the situation, Kirk Cousins was Minnesota’s first choice.

The Vikings explored other options. Multiple players, including fellow former Vikings quarterback Daniel Jones.

Cousins’s history with the team makes it easier to get him up to speed. He had 25 regular-season games with coach Kevin O’Connell, and four seasons with receiver Justin Jefferson.

The existence of options may have helped the Vikings keep the compensation to only a third-round pick. Given the level of desperation, the Vikings seemed destined to give up more than that to fix a problem that threatened to implode the season.

In the end, they managed to stop the bleeding by sending their third-round pick (their own, not the one they acquired from the Eagles for defensive end Jonathan Greenard) to the Raiders for Cousins.

It’s unclear how much better things will be with Cousins back in the fold. It can’t get worse than what they put on the field for Week 9.

After Sunday’s loss to the Giants — Philadelphia’s seventh loss in a row — a video emerged of Eagles coach Nick Sirianni leaving the field, along with Dom DiSandro. There’s one other relevant factor, based on my own personal experience. My dad used to use that exact same move, during periodic verbal household disagreements with my mom.

I saw it. She never did. But that was the move. And, if she’d seen it, he would have denied it.

#2733. NFL Secret Superstars: 3 under-the-radar AFC standouts from Week 9

Superstars may lead the discussion when it comes to success in the NFL, but more often than not, it's the guys you barely know who will make the plays needed when they're needed. Such players can be underrated for all kinds of reasons. Perhaps they're playing in a smaller market, and the media isn't appropriately trumpeting their exploits. Maybe they're hidden in a depth chart behind bigger names who may not be doing as much. Or, it's always possible that a wayward coach hasn't quite figured out that one guy needs elevation to bring better results to the team.

Whatever the reason, we at SB Nation always like to bring the underrated names to you with the offseason Hidden Gems and in-season Secret Superstars series. You can also catch up on the AFC and NFC Secret Superstars for the 2026 season right here.

Here are our AFC Secret Superstars for Week 9 of the 2026 regular season. And if you haven't yet, download the new SB Nation app, so you can better read our stuff and join the conversation wherever you are!

(All advanced metrics courtesy of Next Gen Stats, Pro Football Focus, Sports Info Solutions, and Sumer Sports unless otherwise indicated).

Is your offense better and more dangerous with a truly explosive running back? Obviously, yes. If you have a Bijan Robinson, Jahmyr Gibbs, or James Cook, your offense is going to cook in ways others can't if they don't have one of those rare backs who's always a threat to take one to the house.

But if you don't have such a back — and there are only a handful in the NFL at any given time — what do you do then? The second-best option is to have at least one back who can sustain drives with consistent power, and whatever agility and straight-line speed is in the skill set.

This is why Sean Payton's Denver Broncos selected Washington back Jonah Coleman with the 108th overall pick in the fourth round of the 2026 draft. In his final college season, the 5'9", 228-pound Coleman ran the ball 157 times for 758 yards (4.8 yards per carry), 15 touchdowns, 37 forced missed tackles, 20 runs of 10 or more yards, and 11 runs of 15 or more yards. But all anybody wanted to talk about with Coleman was his alleged lack of explosiveness and burst, which I found interesting based on my own tape study. The guy wasn't running 4.2 40-yard dashes on the field, but big plays happened pretty frequently.

Coleman also caught 31 passes on 34 targets for 346 yards and two touchdowns in 2025, which set him up well for Payton's offense, where the backs need to be receivers.

Coleman's NFL transition was slowed by an ankle injury that landed him on injured reserve in late September, but he's developed into a force multiplier over the last two weeks. He had 104 total yards on 15 touches against the Kansas City Chiefs in a 28-24 Week 8 win, and last Sunday against the Carolina Panthers in a 26-21 win, Coleman ran 17 times for 57 yards and a touchdown, with three forced missed tackles. He also caught four passes for 31 yards. Did he have a single play over 10 yards? No he didn't, but over and over when he was handed the rock, Coleman was able to make Denver's drives easier by creating first downs, and making first downs easier for others by getting the Broncos in favorable down-and-distance situations.

"We had those glimpses in the spring and in training camp and in the preseason," Payton said of Coleman on Nov. 4. "The one thing that was unique about him is oftentimes, you don't get to see [young running backs] in third-down protection plans, but he was someone that we had quite a bit of tape on. Advanced protection plans, and he's built to handle the blitz. It's one thing to know who I have, but then also to be strong enough to do it. I would say that there were three backs [in the draft] — we just saw one of them [Coleman] that we really had visions for. Each one may be a little different, but his versatility, you don't get to stamp that with a lot of rookie backs that you feel like they're going to develop into. We saw enough that we felt like he was going to be a good third-down protecting back as well with the way he was built."

"I think he's playing really well with confidence. He's a very intelligent young running back. What sticks out the most is he can handle a lot. You can put a lot on his plate. After we learned that R.J. Harvey wasn't going to play last week, he got a little bit more of the bulk of R.J.'s stuff. It's impressive to see him be able to be used in a lot of different ways. When he gets in there, he's a natural runner. That's what he's drafted for. That's the position he plays. It's the out of the backfield stuff that I think is making a good impression on us right now."

Smart coaches and personnel people will tell you to avoid focusing on what a player can't do — instead, focus on the potential. Jonah Coleman won't blow you away with RedZone highlights, but when it comes to value for his offense, that's relatively irrelevant — and it's great that he's landed with a team that understands.

After the Texans blew the Los Angeles Chargers out of SoFi Stadium in their 44-22 shellacking of the NFL's only winless team, the conversation turned to quarterback C.J. Stroud's amazing day (21 of 35 for 385 yards, five touchdowns, one interception, and a passer rating of 125.5), Tank Dell's first touchdown catch since Week 16 of the 2024 season, and Nico Collins' insane touchdown catch over cornerback Tarheeb Still.

But the Texan who caught two touchdown passes in that game was Kayshon Boutte, the former New England Patriot who was traded to Houston on Aug. 24 for safety Jaylen Reed and a 2028 seventh-round draft pick. Boutte hadn't been one of the Texans' primary targets this season — he had 15 catches on 25 targets for 194 yards and no touchdowns on the season before Sunday — and head coach DeMeco Ryans was quite happy about Boutte breaking out with four catches on five targets for 125 yards and those two scores.

"I've really been working with the offense trying to get them to get Boutte a pass where he can stay up and run it in for a touchdown—he did that today," Ryans said postgame. "A lot of his catches he's made in the previous weeks were tough catches on the sideline, but this time he was actually able to run one for a touchdown. On that particular play to see [WR] Nico [Collins] down the field blocking and see [TE] Dalton [Schultz] down the field blocking, that's probably what I'm most proud of on that play. Guys really selling out for each other."

Well, now the 3-5 Texans have that to add to the overall arsenal as they try to overcome their unexpectedly rough (0-4) start to the season.

The Jaguars are the best team people aren't talking about enough (they currently rank first in overall DVOA for the first time in franchise history), and a primary reason for that is a defense that is the NFL's most underrated. Anthony Campanile's defense (remember that name; Campanile is on a head coach track) ranked third in Defensive DVOA before they traded for Atlanta Falcons safety Jessie Bates III, New Orleans Saints IDL Davon Godchaux, and Philadelphia Eagles IDL Jordan Davis.

Jags general manager James Gladstone learned all about "F Them Picks" during his time in the Los Angeles Rams' front office from 2016 through 2024, and he has good reason to go all out now. This is a legitimate Super Bowl contender, and the guys that Campanile already had on his roster have played above their name recognition.

Perhaps the man whose national awareness lags farthest behind his actual achievements is linebacker Ventrell Miller. The 2023 fourth-round pick out of Florida lost his entire rookie season to a torn Achilles tendon in the preseason, and had to overcome other ailments in 2024 and 2025, but the fully healthy version of Mr. Miller has been quite the revelation. On the season, Miller has a sack, eight pressures, 35 solo tackles, 23 stops, three tackles for loss, and a forced fumble. In coverage, he's allowed 17 catches on 24 targets for 128 yards, no touchdowns, no interceptions, four pass breakups, and an opponent passer rating of 83.3.

Miller was at his best in last Thursday's 33-10 win over the Baltimore Ravens, showing his skills at the line of scrimmage, and at the second level.

"He's done a really good job overall," Campanile said of Miller back on Sept. 24. "He's a guy you can rely on to set the tone physically for the defense. And that's one thing that I feel like he's done over the first two games. He's looking to improve, he's trying to do extra. And I think all of his teammates see that, and that's been a good thing for our defense."

An extremely good thing for a Jaguars defense that is on the way up. Ventrell Miller has become a top-tier NFL linebacker at the perfect time for his team.

#2732. Recapping the NFL trade deadline for the Broncos

The Denver Broncos ended up adding one new player ahead of the NFL's trade deadline on Tuesday, acquiring linebacker Daiyan Henley through a trade with the Los Angeles Chargers.

The Broncos also explored options at tight end, safety and running back, with Jonathan Taylor, Tony Pollard and Trey McBride among the big-name players Denver checked in on.

The Broncos also had conversations with other clubs about in-house running back J.K. Dobbins and tight end Evan Engram, but both of those players ended up remaining in Denver.

In addition to adding Henley, the Broncos could get several reinforcements back from injuries after their Week 10 bye.

There is hope that wide receiver Jaylen Waddle (concussion), safety Brandon Jones (hamstring), cornerback Riley Moss (concussion), safety Talanoa Hufanga (ankle) and cornerback Kris Abrams-Draine (ankle) could return in time for a Week 11 showdown with the Las Vegas Raiders.

Additionally, defensive lineman Eyioma Uwazurike (shoulder) does not have a season-ending injury, so he is expected to return from injured reserve later this season. Denver running backs Tyler Badie (thumb) and RJ Harvey (knee) are eligible to return from IR in Weeks 12 and 13, respectively. And outside linebacker Jonah Elliss (ankle) is also eligible to return in Week 12.

So, more reinforcements are on the way for the 7-2 Broncos following their mid-season break.

#2731. Houston Rockets vs. Golden State Warriors game preview

They say "It's not who you play; it's when you play them." Well, this isn't when you want to play the Golden State Warriors. Much like how Steph Curry returned from an absence of over two months last season just to play the Rockets, it seems like it's never a good time to see the San Francisco squad.

In case you haven't been following the NBA at large, the Warriors dropped their first three games. Since then, they've reeled off eight straight wins, including wins over the Nuggets in Denver (yes, Nikola Jokic played), the Timberwolves in Minnesota (no, Anthony Edwards did not), and at home against the Los Angeles Lakers (yes, all the white guys played). They've also handily beaten the Clippers, Heat, and Pelicans.

The Warriors basically found out that Kristaps Porzingis was done with basketball around the same time they realized that Yaxel Lendeborg might finally fulfill the "two timelines" prophecy. Lendeborg has emerged as a legitimate Rookie of the Year candidate (and you could easily argue he should be leading the race at the moment) and has made plenty of people forget that the Warriors are still missing Jimmy Butler. If Yaxel continues on this trajectory, the Warriors suddenly become quite terrifying at the deadline armed with Butler and Porzingis as trade chips.

Look, the Warriors are going to play extra well against the Rockets. They always do. There is something in their franchise's DNA that tells them that no matter what, they will play their best basketball against Houston. But the Warriors are playing well right now no matter what, and with the game being on national television, it's another chance for Steph Curry, Draymond Green, and the new kid on the block to torment my favorite basketball squad. Do I have PTSD? You're damn right I do. Honestly, I have no idea why Houston is favored in this one.

The Warriors are starting a five-game road trip against the Southwest Division. If Houston doesn't get them tonight, they will have to get through the streaking Spurs on Friday, a New Orleans team that exists, and then a Dallas-Memphis back-to-back next week. Three of those games are on television (the ones against the Texas teams), so expect Steve Kerr to rest his guys against New Orleans and Memphis. Houston has an extended road trip of their own starting next week. We're officially getting into the grind.

#2730. CBA allows 49ers to not pay Brandon Aiyuk during two-week roster exemption

When unusual situations emerge in the NFL, it becomes a piece-by-piece exercise in figuring out the specific rules that apply. And few situations have been more unusual than 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk’s odyssey of the past year.

He’s back on the 49ers, at a salary of $13.75 million for the rest of the season. That fact pointed to the 49ers releasing him by 4:00 p.m. ET on Tuesday, in order to avoid owing him one-ninth of the total amount ($1.52 million) for Week 10.

But here’s where yet another obscure wrinkle applies. Under Article 20, Section 4 of the Collective Bargaining Agreement, the 49ers owe Aiyuk nothing during his two-week roster exemption.

From the relevant provision: “If a Club obtains a roster exemption for a player under contract who does not report to his Club at least five days before the final day of Preseason Training Camp as defined in Article 23, Section 10, the player will not be entitled to preseason or regular season compensation until such exemption is removed, provided the player is given written notice of such fact upon reporting to the Club. If such notice is not given to the player, the player must be paid his Paragraph 5 Salary during his exemption.”

So if the 49ers gave Aiyuk written notice that he won’t be paid during the two-week roster exemption (and they surely did), they don’t have to pay him. Which pushes their deadline for avoiding owing him a game check until Tuesday, November 24 — one day after the expiration of the exemption.

Why not just cut him? The 49ers are delaying the move because they can, postponing by up to two weeks his passage through waivers and, given his salary, inevitable free agency.

In the interim, he’s on the team. But Aiyuk won’t be reporting for work on Wednesday. He has been excused, we’re told, from team activities and he’s working out on his own.

The 49ers could release him at any moment. There’s no reason not to do so, other than to apply the brakes to his ability to join another team.

The circumstances counter the notion that he was reinstated by the league subject to any type of agreement with the 49ers. If that had happened, the agreement surely would have included a requirement that the 49ers promptly cut him.

Which means that the NFL, after apparently working with the 49ers to delay Aiyuk’s reinstatement in order to help the 49ers recover bonus money from Aiyuk, finally decided that reinstatement from the reserve/left squad list can’t be delayed in order to advance a team’s strategic objectives.

So, yes, Aiyuk eventually will be released. He wasn’t released today because the 49ers don’t owe him a game check for Week 10, or for Week 11. Eventually, when the roster exemption expires, they’ll cut him.

For now, the team’s delay in doing the inevitable points to a desire to keep another team from getting him, sticking it to Aiyuk, or some of both. It’s petty, but the 49ers have the power to do it.

#2729. Aaron Rodgers is one win away from tying Drew Brees for fourth-most of all time

If the Steelers beat the Bengals on Monday night, Aaron Rodgers will move into a tie for fourth time on the NFL’s all-time wins list.

Rodgers’ teams have won 180 games he has started, counting the playoffs. His next win will give him No. 181, the same number Drew Brees retired with, and the fourth most of all time.

The only quarterbacks in NFL history who have won more starts are Tom Brady with a preposterous 286 wins, Peyton Manning with 200 and Brett Favre with 199. Rodgers has said this will be his last NFL season, so he won’t catch Favre for third place.

Rodgers won 158 games as starting quarterback for the Packers, six with the Jets and has now won 16 with the Steelers.
